*Example sentence:* Ich habe Fieber und muss im Bett bleiben. (I have a fever and have to stay in bed.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich habe Fieber* = I have a fever (*ich* = I, *habe* = have [from *haben*], *Fieber* = fever [uncountable neuter noun used without article]).
  - *und muss im Bett bleiben* = and [I] must stay in bed (*und* = and, *muss* = must/have to [modal *müssen*], *im Bett* = in bed [*in + dem Bett*, dative location], *bleiben* = to remain/stay [infinitive at clause end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have a fever and have to stay in bed." Typical excuse note content for school or work.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + irregular verb *haben* (*habe*, position 2) + direct object in accusative (*Fieber*, neuter noun without article as an uncountable condition).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Very common symptom in *Schreiben Teil 2* excuse letters when explaining illness to an employer or language school.

**#91. Mein Bauch tut weh.** — My stomach hurts.
*Example sentence:* Mein Bauch tut weh, ich habe zu viel gegessen. (My stomach hurts, I ate too much.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Bauch tut weh* = My stomach / belly hurts (Lit. "My belly does hurt": *mein Bauch* = my stomach/belly [masculine nominative subject], *tut ... weh* = hurts/aches [separable verb *wehtun*]).
  - *ich habe zu viel gegessen* = I ate too much (*ich* = I, *habe ... gegessen* = have eaten [Perfekt tense of strong verb *essen* with auxiliary *haben*], *zu viel* = too much).
  - *Full meaning:* "My stomach hurts, I ate too much." Everyday complaint after heavy meals.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject in nominative (*mein Bauch*, masculine with possessive *mein*) + separable verb *wehtun* (*tut* in position 2, prefix *weh* at the end).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** A1 body parts vocabulary (*der Bauch*, *der Kopf*, *der Rücken*, *das Bein*, *der Arm*). Used in *Sprechen Teil 2/3* health roleplays.

**#92. Ich habe Husten.** — I have a cough.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe Husten und Schnupfen. (I have a cough and a cold/runny nose.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich habe Husten und Schnupfen* = I have a cough and a cold / runny nose (*ich* = I, *habe* = have, *Husten* = cough [masculine noun without article], *und* = and, *Schnupfen* = head cold / runny nose [masculine noun without article]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have a cough and a cold." The classic pair of cold symptoms described when visiting an *Apotheke* (pharmacy).
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *haben* (*habe*, position 2) + masculine noun without article (*Husten*). Paired frequently with *Schnupfen* (runny nose).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential pharmacy/doctor vocabulary for *Hören* and *Lesen Teil 1* (reading simple medical notices or doctor opening hours).

**#93. Ich muss zum Arzt gehen.** — I have to go to the doctor.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin seit drei Tagen krank, ich muss zum Arzt gehen. (I have been sick for three days, I have to go to the doctor.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin seit drei Tagen krank* = I have been sick for three days (*ich bin* = I am, *seit drei Tagen* = for three days [preposition *seit* takes dative plural; German uses present tense *ich bin* where English requires present perfect "I have been"], *krank* = sick).
  - *ich muss zum Arzt gehen* = I have to go to the doctor (*ich muss* = I must [modal *müssen*], *zum Arzt* = to the doctor [*zu + dem Arzt*, dative masculine contraction], *gehen* = to go [infinitive at clause end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have been sick for three days, I have to go to the doctor." Common dialogue at an appointment booking desk.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal verb bracket: modal verb *müssen* (*muss*, position 2) + directional prepositional phrase with dative contraction (*zu + dem = zum* + dative masculine noun *Arzt*) + main infinitive at sentence end (*gehen*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Tests modal verb sentence bracket (*Satzklammer*) in *Schreiben* and *Sprechen*. Remember: *zum Arzt* (to the doctor - person) vs. *ins Krankenhaus* (to the hospital - building).

**#94. Haben Sie Schmerzen?** — Do you have pain?
*Example sentence:* Haben Sie Schmerzen? Wo tut es weh? (Do you have pain? Where does it hurt?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Haben Sie Schmerzen?* = Do you have pain? (*haben Sie* = have you [formal question order], *Schmerzen* = pains/aches [plural noun without article]).
  - *Wo tut es weh?* = Where does it hurt? (*wo* = where [question word], *tut ... weh* = does hurt [separable *wehtun*], *es* = it [impersonal subject]).
  - *Full meaning:* "Do you have pain? Where does it hurt?" Standard medical diagnostic questions asked by doctors or nurses.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Yes/No question with inverted verb-first word order: verb *haben* (position 1) + formal subject pronoun *Sie* (capitalized, position 2) + plural accusative noun *Schmerzen* without article.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Key question in doctor-patient dialogues tested in *Hören Teil 1 & 2*. Formal *Sie* is mandatory in medical consultations.

**#95. Ich brauche Medizin.** — I need medicine.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin erkältet und brauche Medizin. (I have a cold and need medicine.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin erkältet* = I have caught a cold / I am suffering from a cold (*ich bin* = I am, *erkältet* = suffering from a cold [participial adjective from *sich erkälten*]).
  - *und brauche Medizin* = and [I] need medicine (*und* = and, *brauche* = need, *Medizin* = medicine/pharmaceuticals [feminine noun without article]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have a cold and need medicine." Typical customer opening line at a German pharmacy.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *brauchen* (*brauche*, position 2) + feminine noun *Medizin* (accusative singular without article).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard prompt in *Sprechen Teil 3* (Apotheke card). You can also say *"Ich brauche Tabletten"* (I need pills) or *"Haben Sie Hustensaft?"* (Do you have cough syrup?).

**#96. Mein Arm ist gebrochen.** — My arm is broken.
*Example sentence:* Mein Arm ist gebrochen, ich kann nicht schreiben. (My arm is broken, I cannot write.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Arm ist gebrochen* = My arm is broken (*mein Arm* = my arm [masculine subject], *ist* = is, *gebrochen* = broken [Partizip II of irregular verb *brechen* used as predicate adjective]).
  - *ich kann nicht schreiben* = I cannot write (*ich kann* = I can [modal *können*], *nicht* = not [negation adverb], *schreiben* = to write [infinitive at clause end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My arm is broken, I cannot write." Practical reason given in written communications or exam deferrals.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*mein Arm*, masculine nominative) + copula *sein* (*ist*, position 2) + participle II functioning as predicate adjective (*gebrochen*, from strong verb *brechen*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Useful in *Schreiben Teil 2* excuses for why you cannot attend a sports course or write an exam.

**#97. Ich fühle mich besser.** — I feel better.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe die Tablette genommen und fühle mich besser. (I took the pill and feel better.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich habe die Tablette genommen* = I took the pill (*ich habe ... genommen* = I have taken [Perfekt of strong verb *nehmen*], *die Tablette* = the pill/tablet [feminine accusative direct object]).
  - *und fühle mich besser* = and [I] feel better (*und* = and, *fühle mich* = feel myself [reflexive verb *sich fühlen* + accusative *mich*], *besser* = better [comparative of *gut*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I took the pill and feel better." Describing health recovery.
- **Grammar & Structure:** True reflexive verb *sich fühlen*: subject (*ich*) + conjugated verb *fühle* (position 2) + reflexive pronoun in accusative (*mich*) + comparative adjective (*besser*, comparative of *gut*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Demonstrates mastery of basic reflexive verbs (*sich fühlen*) in *Sprechen Teil 1* and everyday conversational exchanges.

**#98. Gute Besserung!** — Get well soon!
*Example sentence:* Du bist krank? Gute Besserung! (You are sick? Get well soon!)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Du bist krank?* = You are sick? (*du* = you [informal], *bist* = are [from *sein*], *krank* = sick).
  - *Gute Besserung!* = Get well soon! (Lit. "Good recovery / improvement!": *gute* = good [feminine adjective ending *-e*], *Besserung* = betterment/recovery). Short for *"Ich wünsche dir eine gute Besserung"*.
  - *Full meaning:* "You are sick? Get well soon!" The universal German expression of sympathy to someone who is ill.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Formulaic greeting/wish: feminine adjective with strong accusative ending *-e* (*gute*) + feminine noun (*Besserung*). Short for: *"Ich wünsche Ihnen/dir eine gute Besserung."*
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Social formula tested in *Sprechen Teil 1* and polite email conclusions in *Schreiben Teil 2* when replying to a sick colleague or friend.

---

## Family and Relationships

**#99. Das ist meine Mutter.** — That is my mother.
*Example sentence:* Das ist meine Mutter, sie heißt Anna. (That is my mother, her name is Anna.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Das ist meine Mutter* = That is my mother (*das* = that/this [demonstrative pronoun], *ist* = is, *meine Mutter* = my mother [feminine nominative with possessive ending *-e*]).
  - *sie heißt Anna* = her name is Anna / she is called Anna (*sie* = she, *heißt* = is called [from *heißen*], *Anna* = proper name).
  - *Full meaning:* "That is my mother, her name is Anna." Introducing a family member.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Demonstrative pronoun *das* + copula *ist* (position 2) + predicate noun in nominative with feminine possessive article (*meine Mutter*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Fundamental for *Sprechen Teil 1* (introducing family members from photos or personal self-introduction).

**#100. Mein Bruder ist zehn Jahre alt.** — My brother is ten years old.
*Example sentence:* Mein Bruder ist zehn Jahre alt und geht in die Schule. (My brother is ten years old and goes to school.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Bruder ist zehn Jahre alt* = My brother is ten years old (*mein Bruder* = my brother [masculine nominative], *ist* = is, *zehn Jahre alt* = ten years old [standard German age formula]).
  - *und geht in die Schule* = and goes to school (*und* = and, *geht* = goes [from *gehen*], *in die Schule* = into the school [two-way preposition *in* + accusative feminine indicating destination]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My brother is ten years old and goes to school." Everyday family description.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*mein Bruder*, masculine nominative) + copula *ist* (position 2) + age expression (*zehn Jahre alt*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Number pronunciation and age expression (*... Jahre alt*) are tested in *Sprechen Teil 1* and *Hören Teil 1*.

**#101. Ich bin verheiratet.** — I am married.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin verheiratet und habe zwei Kinder. (I am married and have two children.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin verheiratet* = I am married (*ich* = I, *bin* = am, *verheiratet* = married [adjective from *heiraten*]).
  - *und habe zwei Kinder* = and have two children (*und* = and, *habe* = have, *zwei Kinder* = two children [plural of *das Kind*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am married and have two children." Personal profile information in A1 speaking tests.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *sein* (*bin*, position 2) + participle/adjective describing marital status (*verheiratet*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard question/response in *Sprechen Teil 1* personal profile (*Familienstand*: ledig / verheiratet / geschieden).

**#102. Mein Vater arbeitet viel.** — My father works a lot.
*Example sentence:* Mein Vater arbeitet viel, er kommt spät nach Hause. (My father works a lot, he comes home late.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Vater arbeitet viel* = My father works a lot (*mein Vater* = my father [masculine subject], *arbeitet* = works [stem *arbeit-* + connecting vowel *-e-* + 3rd person *-t*], *viel* = a lot/much).
  - *er kommt spät nach Hause* = he comes home late (*er* = he, *kommt* = comes, *spät* = late, *nach Hause* = [towards] home [fixed idiom for movement towards home]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My father works a lot, he comes home late." Describing a family member's daily routine.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*mein Vater*, masculine nominative) + verb *arbeiten* (3rd person singular with connecting *-e-*: *arbeitet*, position 2) + adverb of degree (*viel*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Pay attention to verbs whose stems end in *-t* or *-d* (like *arbeiten*, *finden*) adding an *-e-* before the *-t* ending in 3rd person singular.

**#103. Ich habe eine Schwester.** — I have a sister.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe eine Schwester, sie wohnt in Berlin. (I have a sister, she lives in Berlin.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich habe eine Schwester* = I have a sister (*ich habe* = I have, *eine Schwester* = a sister [feminine accusative indefinite article *-e*]).
  - *sie wohnt in Berlin* = she lives in Berlin (*sie* = she, *wohnt* = resides/lives [from *wohnen*], *in Berlin* = in Berlin [preposition *in* + city name]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have a sister, she lives in Berlin." Expressing family ties and geography.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + transitive verb *haben* (*habe*, position 2) + direct object in feminine accusative (*eine Schwester*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Frequent talking point in *Sprechen Teil 1* under the topic *Familie*.

**#104. Meine Großeltern wohnen auf dem Land.** — My grandparents live in the countryside.
*Example sentence:* Meine Großeltern wohnen auf dem Land, wir besuchen sie oft. (My grandparents live in the countryside, we visit them often.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Meine Großeltern wohnen auf dem Land* = My grandparents live in the countryside (*meine Großeltern* = my grandparents [plural subject], *wohnen* = live/reside, *auf dem Land* = in the countryside / on the land [*auf* + dative neuter *dem Land*]).
  - *wir besuchen sie oft* = we visit them often (*wir* = we, *besuchen* = visit, *sie* = them [3rd person plural accusative pronoun], *oft* = often).
  - *Full meaning:* "My grandparents live in the countryside, we visit them often." Describing lifestyle and family visits.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Plural subject (*meine Großeltern*, plural nominative) + plural verb *wohnen* (position 2) + two-way preposition with dative for location (*auf + dem Land*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Contrasting *in der Stadt* (in the city) vs. *auf dem Land* (in the countryside) is common in *Lesen Teil 2* and *Hören Teil 2*.

**#105. Hast du Kinder?** — Do you have children?
*Example sentence:* Hast du Kinder? Wie alt sind sie? (Do you have children? How old are they?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Hast du Kinder?* = Do you have children? (*hast du* = have you [informal verb-first question], *Kinder* = children [plural of *das Kind* without article]).
  - *Wie alt sind sie?* = How old are they? (*wie alt* = how old, *sind* = are [plural verb], *sie* = they [3rd person plural subject pronoun]).
  - *Full meaning:* "Do you have children? How old are they?" Friendly social inquiry about family.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Informal Yes/No question: verb *haben* in position 1 (*hast*) + informal subject *du* (position 2) + plural accusative direct object (*Kinder* without article). Formal equivalent: *"Haben Sie Kinder?"*.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Crucial question in *Sprechen Teil 2* when asking your partner about their family using a prompt card with the word "Kinder".

**#106. Meine Familie ist groß.** — My family is big.
*Example sentence:* Meine Familie ist groß, ich habe vier Geschwister. (My family is big, I have four siblings.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Meine Familie ist groß* = My family is big (*meine Familie* = my family [grammatically singular feminine noun in German, taking singular verb *ist*], *groß* = big/large).
  - *ich habe vier Geschwister* = I have four siblings (*ich habe* = I have, *vier Geschwister* = four siblings [collective plural noun for brothers and sisters]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My family is big, I have four siblings." Talking about family size.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*meine Familie*, singular feminine noun) + singular copula verb (*ist*, position 2) + predicate adjective (*groß*). Note: *Familie* takes a singular verb in German, unlike in British English.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common mistake: candidates often mistakenly use a plural verb (*meine Familie sind* - WRONG). Always say *meine Familie ist groß*.

**#107. Ich bin ledig.** — I am single / unmarried.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin ledig und lebe allein. (I am single and live alone.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin ledig* = I am single / unmarried (*ich bin* = I am, *ledig* = unmarried/single [formal administrative status term]).
  - *und lebe allein* = and live alone (*und* = and, *lebe* = live, *allein* = alone / by myself).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am single and live alone." Standard marital status response on official German forms (*Formulare*).
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *bin* + adjective of marital status (*ledig*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Official registration form vocabulary (*Formular ausfüllen*) in *Schreiben Teil 1* (filling out personal information on an application form).

**#108. Mein Mann kocht gern.** — My husband likes to cook.
*Example sentence:* Mein Mann kocht gern, er macht das Abendessen. (My husband likes to cook, he makes dinner.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Mann kocht gern* = My husband likes to cook (Lit. "My husband cooks gladly": *mein Mann* = my husband, *kocht* = cooks, *gern* = gladly/with pleasure). German expresses liking an activity by placing *gern* after the conjugated verb.
  - *er macht das Abendessen* = he makes dinner (*er* = he, *macht* = makes/prepares, *das Abendessen* = the evening meal / dinner [compound noun: *Abend* + *Essen*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My husband likes to cook, he makes dinner." Discussing household roles and preferences.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*mein Mann*, masculine nominative) + regular verb *kochen* (3rd person singular *kocht*, position 2) + adverb of preference (*gern/gerne*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** German expresses liking an activity with the adverb *gern* after the conjugated verb, NOT with a verb like "to like" (*er mag kochen* is non-standard at A1).

**#109. Meine Frau kommt aus Spanien.** — My wife comes from Spain.
*Example sentence:* Meine Frau kommt aus Spanien, sie spricht Spanisch und Deutsch. (My wife comes from Spain, she speaks Spanish and German.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Meine Frau kommt aus Spanien* = My wife comes from Spain (*meine Frau* = my wife, *kommt* = comes, *aus Spanien* = from Spain [*aus* + country name without article]).
  - *sie spricht Spanisch und Deutsch* = she speaks Spanish and German (*sie* = she, *spricht* = speaks [irregular stem-vowel change *e -> i* in *sprechen*], *Spanisch und Deutsch* = Spanish and German).
  - *Full meaning:* "My wife comes from Spain, she speaks Spanish and German." Describing partner's origin and languages.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*meine Frau*, feminine nominative) + verb of origin *kommen* (*kommt*, position 2) + preposition of origin requiring dative (*aus* + country name without article: *Spanien*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Key pattern in *Sprechen Teil 1* (*Herkunft*: country of origin + languages spoken).

**#110. Wir sind eine kleine Familie.** — We are a small family.
*Example sentence:* Wir sind eine kleine Familie: mein Mann, mein Sohn und ich. (We are a small family: my husband, my son, and I.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Wir sind eine kleine Familie:* = We are a small family: (*wir* = we, *sind* = are, *eine kleine Familie* = a small family [feminine nominative predicate noun with adjective ending *-e*]).
  - *mein Mann, mein Sohn und ich* = my husband, my son, and I (*mein Mann* = my husband, *mein Sohn* = my son, *und ich* = and I).
  - *Full meaning:* "We are a small family: my husband, my son, and I." Concise self-introduction in speaking exams.
- **Grammar & Structure:** 1st person plural subject (*wir*) + verb *sein* (*sind*, position 2) + predicate noun in feminine nominative with adjective ending *-e* (*eine kleine Familie*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Great introductory sentence for family descriptions in *Sprechen Teil 1* or short descriptive paragraphs in *Schreiben*.

---

## Hobbies and Free Time

**#111. Ich spiele gern Fußball.** — I like playing soccer.
*Example sentence:* Am Samstag spiele ich gern Fußball mit meinen Freunden. (On Saturday I like playing soccer with my friends.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Am Samstag* = On Saturday (*an + dem Samstag*, temporal phrase in position 1 triggering subject-verb inversion).
  - *spiele ich gern Fußball* = I like playing soccer (Lit. "play I gladly soccer": *spiele* = play, *ich* = I, *gern* = gladly, *Fußball* = soccer/football [sports take no article]).
  - *mit meinen Freunden* = with my friends (*mit* = with [always requires dative], *meinen Freunden* = my friends [plural dative possessive *-en* + noun plural dative *-n*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "On Saturday I like playing soccer with my friends." Expressing weekend leisure activities.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + regular verb *spielen* (position 2) + adverb *gern* + direct object (*Fußball*). In the example sentence, inversion occurs due to time element in position 1 (*Am Samstag*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Top topic in *Sprechen Teil 1 & 2* under "Hobbys / Sport". Remember sports do not take an article (*Fußball spielen*, *Tennis spielen*).

**#112. Ich lese gern Bücher.** — I like reading books.
*Example sentence:* Ich lese gern Bücher, besonders Krimis. (I like reading books, especially crime novels.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich lese gern Bücher* = I like reading books (Lit. "I read gladly books": *ich* = I, *lese* = read [from irregular verb *lesen*], *gern* = gladly, *Bücher* = books [plural of *das Buch*]).
  - *besonders Krimis* = especially crime novels / mystery stories (*besonders* = particularly/especially, *Krimis* = detective/crime stories [colloquial abbreviation of *Kriminalromane*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I like reading books, especially crime novels." Talking about literary interests.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + stem-changing irregular verb *lesen* (1st person *lese*, 2nd/3rd person *liest*, position 2) + adverb *gern* + plural noun object (*Bücher*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Highlighting stem-changing verbs (*e -> ie* in *du liest / er liest*) is essential for the A1 grammar requirement.

**#113. Am Wochenende gehe ich ins Kino.** — On the weekend I go to the cinema.
*Example sentence:* Am Wochenende gehe ich ins Kino, der Film beginnt um acht. (On the weekend I go to the cinema, the movie starts at eight.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Am Wochenende gehe ich ins Kino* = On the weekend I go to the cinema (*am Wochenende* = on the weekend [*an + dem*], *gehe ich* = go I [inversion], *ins Kino* = into the movie theater [*in + das Kino*, two-way preposition with accusative for destination]).
  - *der Film beginnt um acht* = the movie starts at eight (*der Film* = the film/movie, *beginnt* = begins, *um acht* = at eight [preposition *um* for clock time]).
  - *Full meaning:* "On the weekend I go to the cinema, the movie starts at eight." Making plans for weekend entertainment.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Inversion triggered by adverbial phrase in position 1 (*Am Wochenende* = *an + dem*) + verb in position 2 (*gehe*) + subject in position 3 (*ich*) + directional preposition contraction in accusative (*in + das = ins Kino*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** German V2 rule: when time comes first (*Am Wochenende*), verb MUST remain in second position, pushing the subject to third position.

**#114. Ich höre gern Musik.** — I like listening to music.
*Example sentence:* Ich höre gern Musik, am liebsten Pop. (I like listening to music, preferably pop.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich höre gern Musik* = I like listening to music (*ich* = I, *höre* = hear/listen to, *gern* = gladly, *Musik* = music [feminine noun used without article]).
  - *am liebsten Pop* = most of all pop music (*am liebsten* = most preferably / best of all [superlative of *gern*], *Pop* = pop music).
  - *Full meaning:* "I like listening to music, preferably pop." Stating musical tastes.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + regular verb *hören* (*höre*, position 2) + adverb *gern* + uncountable feminine noun (*Musik* without article).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Frequent question card in *Sprechen Teil 2* (Card: "Musik" / "Freizeit"). Question: *"Hören Sie gern Musik?"* Answer: *"Ja, sehr gern."*

**#115. Ich koche gern.** — I like to cook.
*Example sentence:* Ich koche gern, heute mache ich Pasta. (I like to cook, today I'm making pasta.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich koche gern* = I like cooking / to cook (*ich* = I, *koche* = cook, *gern* = gladly).
  - *heute mache ich Pasta* = today I am making pasta (*heute* = today [time element in position 1], *mache ich* = make I [verb-second inversion], *Pasta* = pasta).
  - *Full meaning:* "I like to cook, today I'm making pasta." Cooking hobby and meal preparation.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *kochen* (position 2) + adverb *gern*. Intransitive usage here.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Great simple answer in *Sprechen Teil 1* when the examiner asks *"Was sind Ihre Hobbys?"* (*"Meine Hobbys sind Kochen und Lesen"* or *"Ich koche und lese gern"*).

**#116. Ich gehe gern spazieren.** — I like going for a walk.
*Example sentence:* Am Sonntag gehe ich gern im Park spazieren. (On Sunday I like going for a walk in the park.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Am Sonntag gehe ich gern im Park spazieren* = On Sunday I like going for a walk in the park (*am Sonntag* = on Sunday, *gehe ich* = go I, *gern* = gladly, *im Park* = in the park [*in + dem*, dative location where strolling occurs], *spazieren* = strolling [verbal complement at sentence end creating idiom *spazieren gehen*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "On Sunday I like going for a walk in the park." Describing outdoor leisure routines.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Verbal compound: conjugated motion verb *gehen* (position 2) + infinitive *spazieren* placed at the end of the clause. Dative location in between: *im Park* (*in + dem*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Demonstrates dynamic + static spatial distinction. *Im Park* = where the walking takes place (dative).

**#117. Ich schwimme gern.** — I like to swim.
*Example sentence:* Im Sommer schwimme ich gern im See. (In summer I like to swim in the lake.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Im Sommer schwimme ich gern im See* = In summer I like to swim in the lake (*im Sommer* = in summer [*in + dem*], *schwimme ich* = swim I [inversion], *gern* = gladly, *im See* = in the lake [*in + dem See*, masculine dative location]).
  - *Full meaning:* "In summer I like to swim in the lake." Describing seasonal sports activities.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Inverted word order with temporal phrase in position 1 (*Im Sommer* = *in + dem Sommer*) + verb *schwimme* (position 2) + subject *ich* (position 3) + adverb *gern* + dative location (*im See* = *in + dem See*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Preposition *im* is used for seasons (*im Sommer*, *im Winter*) and months (*im Juli*). Regularly tested in *Hören Teil 1*.

**#118. Mein Hobby ist Tanzen.** — My hobby is dancing.
*Example sentence:* Mein Hobby ist Tanzen, ich gehe jeden Mittwoch zum Tanzkurs. (My hobby is dancing, I go to dance class every Wednesday.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Hobby ist Tanzen* = My hobby is dancing (*mein Hobby* = my hobby, *ist* = is, *Tanzen* = dancing [infinitive used as neuter noun, capitalized]).
  - *ich gehe jeden Mittwoch zum Tanzkurs* = I go to dance class every Wednesday (*ich gehe* = I go, *jeden Mittwoch* = every Wednesday [masculine accusative temporal expression], *zum Tanzkurs* = to the dance class [*zu + dem*, dative masculine]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My hobby is dancing, I go to dance class every Wednesday." Detailed hobby explanation.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*mein Hobby*, neuter nominative) + copula *ist* (position 2) + nominalized infinitive (*das Tanzen*, capitalized, neuter).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Perfect phrasing for *Sprechen Teil 1* self-introduction. If mentioning multiple hobbies: *"Meine Hobbys sind Tanzen und Schwimmen."* (plural verb *sind*).

**#119. Ich fahre gern Fahrrad.** — I like riding a bicycle.
*Example sentence:* Bei schönem Wetter fahre ich gern Fahrrad. (In nice weather I like riding a bicycle.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Bei schönem Wetter* = In nice weather (*bei* = at/during [preposition taking dative], *schönem Wetter* = nice weather [neuter dative adjective ending *-em*]).
  - *fahre ich gern Fahrrad* = I like riding a bicycle (*fahre ich* = ride/drive I [inversion], *gern* = gladly, *Fahrrad* = bicycle [verb-noun collocation *Fahrrad fahren*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "In nice weather I like riding a bicycle." Mentioning weather-dependent activities.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Stem-changing strong verb *fahren* (position 2) + adverb *gern* + noun *Fahrrad* (acts as separable element at clause end).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Notice the idiom *Fahrrad fahren* or *Rad fahren*. In 2nd/3rd person: *du fährst*, *er fährt* (a -> ä umlaut change).

**#120. Ich sehe gern fern.** — I like watching TV.
*Example sentence:* Am Abend sehe ich gern fern. (In the evening I like watching TV.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Am Abend sehe ich gern fern* = In the evening I like watching TV (*am Abend* = in the evening [*an + dem*], *sehe ich* = watch I [inversion], *gern* = gladly, *fern* = tele/far [separable prefix of *fernsehen* placed at the end of the clause]).
  - *Full meaning:* "In the evening I like watching TV." Evening relaxation routine.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Separable stem-changing verb *fernsehen*: conjugated stem *sehe* (position 2) + prefix *fern* at the absolute end of the clause. (Irregular: *du siehst fern*, *er sieht fern*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Classic separable verb tested in A1. Never leave the prefix attached to the verb in a main clause (*Ich fernsehe* is WRONG).

**#121. Ich treffe mich mit Freunden.** — I meet up with friends.
*Example sentence:* Am Freitag treffe ich mich mit Freunden im Café. (On Friday I meet up with friends at the café.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Am Freitag treffe ich mich mit Freunden im Café* = On Friday I meet up with friends at the café (*am Freitag* = on Friday, *treffe ich mich* = meet I myself [reflexive verb *sich treffen* with accusative pronoun *mich*], *mit Freunden* = with friends [*mit* + dative plural ending in *-n*], *im Café* = in the café [*in + dem Café*, dative location]).
  - *Full meaning:* "On Friday I meet up with friends at the café." Social plans with friends.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Reflexive verb *sich treffen*: conjugated verb *treffe* (position 2) + reflexive pronoun in accusative (*mich*) + preposition requiring dative (*mit* + plural dative *Freunden* with plural dative *-n*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Remind learners that *mit* always takes Dative, and plural nouns take an additional *-n* in Dative unless they already end in *-n* or *-s* (*Freunde* -> *mit Freunden*).

**#122. Hast du ein Hobby?** — Do you have a hobby?
*Example sentence:* Hast du ein Hobby? Was machst du in deiner Freizeit? (Do you have a hobby? What do you do in your free time?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Hast du ein Hobby?* = Do you have a hobby? (*hast du* = have you [informal question], *ein Hobby* = a hobby [neuter accusative object]).
  - *Was machst du in deiner Freizeit?* = What do you do in your free time? (*was* = what, *machst du* = do you do, *in deiner Freizeit* = in your free time [preposition *in* + feminine dative possessive *deiner*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "Do you have a hobby? What do you do in your free time?" Standard conversation starter in pair-work exams.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Yes/No question: verb *hast* (position 1) + subject *du* (position 2) + neuter accusative object (*ein Hobby*). Formal equivalent: *"Haben Sie ein Hobby?"*.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard prompt question for *Sprechen Teil 2* card with keyword "Hobby" or "Freizeit".

**#123. Ich spiele gern Gitarre.** — I like playing guitar.
*Example sentence:* Ich spiele gern Gitarre, ich übe jeden Tag. (I like playing guitar, I practice every day.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich spiele gern Gitarre* = I like playing guitar (*ich spiele* = I play, *gern* = gladly, *Gitarre* = guitar [musical instruments do not take an article with *spielen*]).
  - *ich übe jeden Tag* = I practice every day (*ich übe* = I practice [from *üben*], *jeden Tag* = every day [masculine accusative temporal expression]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I like playing guitar, I practice every day." Talking about musical hobbies and dedication.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *spielen* (position 2) + adverb *gern* + musical instrument (*Gitarre*, no article).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** In German, musical instruments do not take a definite article when paired with *spielen* (*Ich spiele Gitarre/Klavier/Geige*).

---

## School and Work

**#124. Ich arbeite als Lehrer.** — I work as a teacher.
*Example sentence:* Ich arbeite als Lehrer an einer Grundschule. (I work as a teacher at an elementary school.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich arbeite als Lehrer an einer Grundschule* = I work as a teacher at an elementary school (*ich arbeite* = I work, *als Lehrer* = as a teacher [preposition *als* indicates job/role, followed by noun without article], *an einer Grundschule* = at an elementary school [*an* + dative feminine location]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I work as a teacher at an elementary school." Introducing professional occupation and workplace.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *arbeiten* (position 2) + professional role preposition *als* + profession noun in nominative without article (*Lehrer* / feminine: *Lehrerin*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard pattern for *Beruf* (profession) in *Sprechen Teil 1*. Always use *arbeiten als [Beruf]* or *Ich bin [Beruf] von Beruf*.

**#125. Ich lerne Deutsch.** — I am learning German.
*Example sentence:* Ich lerne Deutsch seit drei Monaten. (I have been learning German for three months.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich lerne Deutsch seit drei Monaten* = I have been learning German for three months (*ich lerne* = I learn/study, *Deutsch* = German [language without article], *seit drei Monaten* = since/for three months [preposition *seit* + dative plural noun *Monaten* with plural dative *-n*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have been learning German for three months." Stating language learning experience.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + regular verb *lernen* (position 2) + language object without article (*Deutsch*). Follow-up with temporal preposition *seit* (+ dative plural: *drei Monaten*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Core response in *Sprechen Teil 1* when answering why you are taking the Goethe A1 examination.

**#126. Die Schule beginnt um acht Uhr.** — School starts at eight o'clock.
*Example sentence:* Die Schule beginnt um acht Uhr, ich stehe um sieben auf. (School starts at eight o'clock, I get up at seven.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Die Schule beginnt um acht Uhr* = School starts at eight o'clock (*die Schule* = the school, *beginnt* = begins/starts, *um acht Uhr* = at eight o'clock [*um* for exact time]).
  - *ich stehe um sieben auf* = I get up at seven (*ich* = I, *stehe ... auf* = get up/stand up [separable verb *aufstehen*], *um sieben* = at seven).
  - *Full meaning:* "School starts at eight o'clock, I get up at seven." Describing morning school routine.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*die Schule*, feminine nominative) + verb *beginnen* (position 2) + exact time preposition with accusative (*um* + *acht Uhr*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Clock times with *um* are tested heavily in *Hören Teil 1 & 2* (distinguishing schedule details like 08:00 vs 08:30).

**#127. Ich bin Student.** — I am a university student.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin Student und studiere Informatik. (I am a university student and study computer science.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin Student* = I am a university student (*ich bin* = I am, *Student* = tertiary/college student [contrast with *Schüler* for K-12 school students]).
  - *und studiere Informatik* = and study computer science (*und* = and, *studiere* = major in / study at university level [from *studieren*], *Informatik* = computer science).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am a university student and study computer science." Academic status introduction.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *sein* (*bin*, position 2) + masculine profession/role noun without article (*Student* / feminine: *Studentin*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Note difference between *Schüler* (school student, K-12) and *Student* (university/college student) and verbs *lernen* (learn/study school material) vs *studieren* (major in university).

**#128. Wo arbeiten Sie?** — Where do you work?
*Example sentence:* Wo arbeiten Sie? Ich arbeite in einem Büro. (Where do you work? I work in an office.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Wo arbeiten Sie?* = Where do you work? (*wo* = where [question word for location], *arbeiten Sie* = work you [formal polite address]).
  - *Ich arbeite in einem Büro* = I work in an office (*ich arbeite* = I work, *in einem Büro* = in an office [*in* + neuter dative indefinite article *einem*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "Where do you work? I work in an office." Polite inquiry and answer regarding workplace.
- **Grammar & Structure:** W-question: question word *Wo* (position 1) + verb *arbeiten* (position 2) + formal subject *Sie* (capitalized, position 3).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Goethe A1 *Sprechen Teil 2* card "Arbeit" / "Beruf". Partner responds with prepositional phrase: *"In einer Bank"*, *"Im Krankenhaus"*, *"Bei Siemens"*.


**#129. Ich habe einen Kurs.** — I have a course / class.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe einen Kurs am Montag und Mittwoch. (I have a course on Monday and Wednesday.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich habe einen Kurs am Montag und Mittwoch* = I have a course on Monday and Wednesday (*ich habe* = I have, *einen Kurs* = a course [masculine accusative direct object], *am Montag und Mittwoch* = on Monday and Wednesday [*an + dem*, standard preposition for days]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have a course on Monday and Wednesday." Scheduling commitments and class days.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *haben* (*habe*, position 2) + masculine direct object in accusative (*einen Kurs*). Days of the week take preposition *am* (*an + dem*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common scheduling context in *Schreiben Teil 2* when requesting or rescheduling appointments with classmates or instructors.

**#130. Der Unterricht ist um drei Uhr zu Ende.** — Class finishes at three o'clock.
*Example sentence:* Der Unterricht ist um drei Uhr zu Ende, dann gehe ich nach Hause. (Class finishes at three o'clock, then I go home.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Der Unterricht ist um drei Uhr zu Ende* = Class finishes at three o'clock (*der Unterricht* = the instruction/class, *ist ... zu Ende* = is finished / at an end [idiom *zu Ende sein*], *um drei Uhr* = at three o'clock).
  - *dann gehe ich nach Hause* = then I go home (*dann* = then [adverb in position 1 causing inversion], *gehe ich* = go I, *nach Hause* = [towards] home).
  - *Full meaning:* "Class finishes at three o'clock, then I go home." Describing the end of a school day.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*der Unterricht*, masculine nominative) + copula *ist* (position 2) + time phrase (*um drei Uhr*) + idiom *zu Ende sein* (to be over / finished).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential listening cue in *Hören Teil 2* regarding course schedules, closing times, and timetables.

**#131. Ich mache eine Ausbildung.** — I am doing an apprenticeship / vocational training.
*Example sentence:* Ich mache eine Ausbildung als Krankenpfleger. (I am doing an apprenticeship as a male nurse.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich mache eine Ausbildung als Krankenpfleger* = I am doing an apprenticeship as a male nurse (*ich mache* = I do/make, *eine Ausbildung* = a vocational training / apprenticeship [feminine accusative], *als Krankenpfleger* = as a male nurse [role noun without article]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am doing an apprenticeship as a male nurse." Describing vocational education in Germany.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *machen* (position 2) + feminine direct object in accusative (*eine Ausbildung*) + preposition *als* + profession.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Very common career status in Germany, frequently appears in *Lesen Teil 1 & 2* short biographies and profile questions.

**#132. Ich schreibe morgen einen Test.** — I am writing a test tomorrow.
*Example sentence:* Ich schreibe morgen einen Test, ich muss noch lernen. (I am writing a test tomorrow, I still have to study.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich schreibe morgen einen Test* = I am writing a test tomorrow (*ich schreibe* = I write/take, *morgen* = tomorrow, *einen Test* = a test [masculine accusative direct object]).
  - *ich muss noch lernen* = I still have to study (*ich muss* = I must [modal *müssen*], *noch* = still/yet, *lernen* = to study/learn [infinitive at clause end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am writing a test tomorrow, I still have to study." Explaining test preparation and busy schedule.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *schreiben* (position 2) + temporal adverb (*morgen*) + masculine direct object in accusative (*einen Test*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** In German, the present tense often expresses near future events when combined with a time marker like *morgen*, *später*, or *nächste Woche*.

**#133. Ich habe viel Arbeit.** — I have a lot of work.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe viel Arbeit, ich kann heute nicht kommen. (I have a lot of work, I cannot come today.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich habe viel Arbeit* = I have a lot of work (*ich habe* = I have, *viel Arbeit* = much work [uninflected *viel* before uncountable feminine noun *Arbeit*]).
  - *ich kann heute nicht kommen* = I cannot come today (*ich kann* = I can [modal *können*], *heute* = today, *nicht* = not, *kommen* = to come [infinitive at clause end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I have a lot of work, I cannot come today." Polite justification for declining an invitation or meeting.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *haben* (*habe*, position 2) + indefinite quantifier (*viel*, uninflected before uncountable nouns) + feminine noun (*die Arbeit*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Perfect justification in *Schreiben Teil 2* emails for declining an invitation (*Einladung ablehnen*).

**#134. Mein Chef ist nett.** — My boss is nice.
*Example sentence:* Mein Chef ist nett, er hilft mir oft. (My boss is nice, he often helps me.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Chef ist nett* = My boss is nice (*mein Chef* = my boss/supervisor [masculine nominative subject], *ist* = is, *nett* = nice/friendly [predicate adjective]).
  - *er hilft mir oft* = he often helps me (*er* = he, *hilft* = helps [stem vowel change *e -> i* in *helfen*], *mir* = me [dative pronoun required by *helfen*], *oft* = often).
  - *Full meaning:* "My boss is nice, he often helps me." Describing a workplace supervisor.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*mein Chef*, masculine nominative) + copula *ist* (position 2) + predicate adjective (*nett*, uninflected after *sein*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Note that predicate adjectives following *sein*, *bleiben*, or *werden* do NOT take adjective endings (*Der Chef ist nett* vs. *ein netter Chef*).

**#135. Ich suche einen Job.** — I am looking for a job.
*Example sentence:* Ich suche einen Job als Verkäufer. (I am looking for a job as a salesperson.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich suche einen Job als Verkäufer* = I am looking for a job as a salesperson (*ich suche* = I seek/search for, *einen Job* = a job [masculine accusative article *-en*], *als Verkäufer* = as a salesperson [role noun]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am looking for a job as a salesperson." Stating job search goals in interviews or classifieds.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + transitive verb *suchen* (position 2) + masculine direct object in accusative (*einen Job*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential in *Lesen Teil 2* when matching job advertisements with job seekers' profiles.

**#136. Ich bin Ärztin von Beruf.** — I am a doctor by profession.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin Ärztin von Beruf und arbeite im Krankenhaus. (I am a doctor by profession and work at the hospital.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin Ärztin von Beruf* = I am a doctor by profession (*ich bin* = I am, *Ärztin* = female physician [suffix *-in* denotes female professional], *von Beruf* = by profession [fixed idiom]).
  - *und arbeite im Krankenhaus* = and work in the hospital (*und* = and, *arbeite* = work, *im Krankenhaus* = in the hospital [*in + dem*, dative neuter location]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am a doctor by profession and work at the hospital." Complete statement of occupation and institution.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *bin* + feminine profession noun (*Ärztin* with suffix *-in*) + fixed prepositional phrase (*von Beruf* = by profession).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard formulaic answer in *Sprechen Teil 1* when asked *"Was sind Sie von Beruf?"* or *"Was machen Sie beruflich?"*.

---

## Feelings and Opinions

**#137. Ich bin müde.** — I am tired.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin müde, ich habe schlecht geschlafen. (I am tired, I slept badly.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin müde* = I am tired (*ich bin* = I am, *müde* = tired/exhausted).
  - *ich habe schlecht geschlafen* = I slept badly (*ich habe ... geschlafen* = I have slept [Perfekt tense of *schlafen* with *haben*], *schlecht* = badly/poorly).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am tired, I slept badly." Everyday explanation for low energy or fatigue.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *sein* (*bin*, position 2) + predicate adjective (*müde*). Follow-up clause uses Perfekt with *haben*: *habe schlecht geschlafen*.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Frequently used in everyday dialogues in *Hören Teil 1* and informal chats explaining why someone wants to leave a party early.

**#138. Das gefällt mir.** — I like that. / That pleases me.
*Example sentence:* Dein neues Kleid ist schön, das gefällt mir. (Your new dress is beautiful, I like that.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Dein neues Kleid ist schön* = Your new dress is beautiful (*dein neues Kleid* = your new dress [neuter subject with mixed adjective ending *-es*], *ist* = is, *schön* = beautiful).
  - *das gefällt mir* = I like that (Lit. "that pleases to-me": *das* = that [demonstrative subject], *gefällt* = pleases [stem-changing verb *gefallen* with *a -> ä*], *mir* = to me [dative pronoun]).
  - *Full meaning:* "Your new dress is beautiful, I like that." Giving a compliment on someone's clothing.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject in nominative (*das*, demonstrative pronoun) + stem-changing verb *gefallen* (position 2, 3rd person singular *gefällt*, *a -> ä*) + dative object (*mir*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Crucial A1 grammatical construction: *gefallen* requires the liked thing as the grammatical subject, and the person liking it as the DATIVE object (*Das gefällt mir / Gefällt dir das?*).

**#139. Ich finde das toll.** — I think that's great.
*Example sentence:* Du hast die Prüfung bestanden? Ich finde das toll! (You passed the exam? I think that's great!)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Du hast die Prüfung bestanden?* = You passed the exam? (*du hast ... bestanden* = you have passed [Perfekt of *bestehen*], *die Prüfung* = the examination [feminine accusative]).
  - *Ich finde das toll!* = I think that's great! (*ich finde* = I find/think, *das* = that, *toll* = fantastic/great).
  - *Full meaning:* "You passed the exam? I think that's great!" Congratulating someone on an achievement.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + opinion verb *finden* (position 2) + accusative pronoun object (*das*) + adjective complement (*toll*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Key phrase for expressing positive personal opinions in *Sprechen Teil 2* dialogues and informal email writing in *Schreiben Teil 2*.

**#140. Ich bin glücklich.** — I am happy.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin glücklich, wir fahren morgen in den Urlaub. (I am happy, we're going on vacation tomorrow.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin glücklich* = I am happy (*ich bin* = I am, *glücklich* = happy/joyful).
  - *wir fahren morgen in den Urlaub* = we are going on vacation tomorrow (*wir fahren* = we drive/go, *morgen* = tomorrow, *in den Urlaub* = on vacation [*in* + accusative masculine indicating departure for holiday]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am happy, we're going on vacation tomorrow." Expressing joy before a trip.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *bin* (position 2) + adjective (*glücklich*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Useful in *Schreiben Teil 2* when responding enthusiastically to positive news or good invitations (*"Ich freue mich sehr / Ich bin so glücklich"*).

**#141. Ich bin traurig.** — I am sad.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin traurig, mein Freund ist weggezogen. (I am sad, my friend moved away.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin traurig* = I am sad (*ich bin* = I am, *traurig* = sad/sorrowful).
  - *mein Freund ist weggezogen* = my friend moved away (*mein Freund* = my friend/boyfriend, *ist ... weggezogen* = has moved away [Perfekt of motion verb *wegziehen* requiring auxiliary *sein*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am sad, my friend moved away." Expressing personal sadness about a departed friend.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *bin* + adjective (*traurig*). Follow-up clause demonstrates Perfekt with *sein* for movement: *ist weggezogen*.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Helps express emotions in A1 basic personal correspondence.

**#142. Ich habe Angst.** — I am afraid. / I am scared.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe Angst vor der Prüfung. (I am afraid of the exam.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich habe Angst vor der Prüfung* = I am afraid of the exam (Lit. "I have fear before the exam": *ich habe* = I have, *Angst* = fear/anxiety, *vor der Prüfung* = of/before the exam [preposition *vor* always takes dative when used with *Angst haben*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am afraid of the exam." Expressing exam anxiety or nervousness.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *haben* (*habe*, position 2) + feminine noun without article (*Angst*) + fixed preposition requiring dative (*vor + der Prüfung*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Idiomatic expression: in German, one "has fear" (*Angst haben*) rather than "being afraid" with *sein* (*Ich bin ängstlich* is rare in everyday A1 German).

**#143. Das ist langweilig.** — That is boring.
*Example sentence:* Der Film ist nicht gut, das ist langweilig. (The movie isn't good, that is boring.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Der Film ist nicht gut* = The movie is not good (*der Film* = the film, *ist* = is, *nicht gut* = not good).
  - *das ist langweilig* = that is boring (*das* = that, *ist* = is, *langweilig* = boring/uninteresting).
  - *Full meaning:* "The movie isn't good, that is boring." Expressing boredom or negative critique.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Demonstrative subject (*das*) + copula *ist* (position 2) + adjective (*langweilig*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Expressing negative assessment or disinterest during *Sprechen Teil 2* conversations about movies, books, or activities.

**#144. Ich bin froh.** — I am glad / relieved.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin froh, dass du hier bist. (I am glad that you are here.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin froh* = I am glad / relieved (*ich bin* = I am, *froh* = glad/pleased).
  - *dass du hier bist* = that you are here (*dass* = that [subordinating conjunction that sends the verb to the end of the clause], *du* = you, *hier* = here, *bist* = are [verb at end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am glad that you are here." Welcoming someone or expressing relief at their presence.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Main clause with subject (*ich*) + copula *bin* + adjective (*froh*). Subordinate clause with conjunction *dass* pushes the conjugated verb (*bist*) to the very end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Shows emotional reaction in *Sprechen Teil 1/2* and *Schreiben Teil 2* thanking someone for their visit or assistance.

**#145. Das finde ich nicht gut.** — I don't think that's good.
*Example sentence:* Du rauchst? Das finde ich nicht gut. (You smoke? I don't think that's good.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Du rauchst?* = You smoke? (*du* = you, *rauchst* = smoke [from *rauchen*]).
  - *Das finde ich nicht gut* = I don't think that's good (Lit. "That find I not good": *das* = that [accusative object in position 1 for emphasis], *finde ich* = find I [inversion], *nicht gut* = not good).
  - *Full meaning:* "You smoke? I don't think that's good." Disapproving of a habit or action.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Inversion for emphasis: accusative object (*das*, position 1) + verb *finde* (position 2) + subject *ich* (position 3) + negation particle *nicht* + adjective (*gut*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Placing *das* at the beginning emphasizes the topic being evaluated. Negating adjectives uses *nicht*, not *kein*.

**#146. Mir ist kalt.** — I am cold.
*Example sentence:* Mir ist kalt, kannst du das Fenster schließen? (I am cold, can you close the window?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mir ist kalt* = I am cold (Lit. "To-me is cold": *mir* = to me [dative experiencer], *ist* = is, *kalt* = cold; remember: never say *Ich bin kalt*, which means "I am dead / cold-hearted").
  - *kannst du das Fenster schließen?* = can you close the window? (*kannst du* = can you [informal polite request], *das Fenster* = the window [neuter accusative], *schließen* = to shut/close [infinitive at end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am cold, can you close the window?" Everyday home or office request.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Impersonal physical sensation: dative pronoun (*mir*) + copula *ist* (position 2) + adjective (*kalt*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Crucial German vs English contrast: *"Ich bin kalt"* means "I am cold-hearted/dead". To say you feel cold, you MUST use dative: *"Mir ist kalt"* (or *"Ist dir kalt?"*).

**#147. Mir ist warm.** — I am warm / hot.
*Example sentence:* Mir ist warm, ich mache das Fenster auf. (I am warm, I'll open the window.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mir ist warm* = I am warm / hot (Lit. "To-me is warm": *mir* = to me [dative experiencer], *ist* = is, *warm* = warm; never say *Ich bin warm*, which has unwanted colloquial connotations).
  - *ich mache das Fenster auf* = I'll open the window (*ich* = I, *mache ... auf* = open [separable verb *aufmachen*], *das Fenster* = the window).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am warm, I'll open the window." Room climate adjustment in daily life.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Impersonal dative pronoun (*mir*) + copula *ist* (position 2) + adjective (*warm*). Follow-up clause has separable verb *aufmachen* (*mache ... auf*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Same as #146: *"Ich bin warm"* has inappropriate idiomatic connotations. Always say *"Mir ist warm"* or *"Mir ist heiß"*.

**#148. Ich bin nervös.** — I am nervous.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin nervös, morgen ist mein Vorstellungsgespräch. (I am nervous, tomorrow is my job interview.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin nervös* = I am nervous (*ich bin* = I am, *nervös* = nervous/anxious).
  - *morgen ist mein Vorstellungsgespräch* = tomorrow is my job interview (*morgen* = tomorrow [temporal adverb in position 1], *ist* = is, *mein Vorstellungsgespräch* = my job interview [compound noun: *Vorstellung* + *Gespräch*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am nervous, tomorrow is my job interview." Pre-interview tension.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + copula *bin* (position 2) + adjective (*nervös*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Great natural sentence to break the ice with the examiners in *Sprechen Teil 1*: *"Entschuldigung, ich bin ein bisschen nervös."*

**#149. Das macht Spaß!** — That's fun!
*Example sentence:* Wir spielen zusammen, das macht Spaß! (We're playing together, that's fun!)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Wir spielen zusammen* = We are playing together (*wir spielen* = we play, *zusammen* = together).
  - *das macht Spaß!* = that's fun! (Lit. "that makes fun": *das* = that, *macht* = makes, *Spaß* = fun/pleasure; the standard German idiom for having fun is *Spaß machen*).
  - *Full meaning:* "We're playing together, that's fun!" Expressing enjoyment during games or activities.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*das*) + transitive verb *machen* (position 2, *macht*) + masculine noun object (*Spaß* without article).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Idiomatic expression: in German, things "make fun" (*das macht Spaß* / *Deutsch lernen macht Spaß*). Do NOT translate literally as *"das ist Spaß"*.

---

## Transportation

**#150. Wo fährt der Bus ab?** — Where does the bus depart?
*Example sentence:* Entschuldigung, wo fährt der Bus ab? Am Bahnhof? (Excuse me, where does the bus depart? At the station?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Entschuldigung* = Excuse me / Pardon me [polite conversational opener].
  - *wo fährt der Bus ab?* = where does the bus depart? (*wo* = where, *fährt ... ab* = departs [from separable verb *abfahren*], *der Bus* = the bus [masculine nominative subject]).
  - *Am Bahnhof?* = At the train station? (*an + dem Bahnhof*, dative location).
  - *Full meaning:* "Excuse me, where does the bus depart? At the station?" Inquiring about public transit departure points.
- **Grammar & Structure:** W-question: question word *Wo* (position 1) + stem-changing separable verb *abfahren* (conjugated stem *fährt* in position 2, prefix *ab* at the end) + subject (*der Bus*, masculine nominative).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard transport query in *Hören Teil 1/2* and *Sprechen Teil 2* card "Verkehr / Bahn / Bus".

**#151. Ich fahre mit dem Zug.** — I travel by train.
*Example sentence:* Ich fahre mit dem Zug nach München. (I travel by train to Munich.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich fahre mit dem Zug nach München* = I travel by train to Munich (*ich fahre* = I travel/ride, *mit dem Zug* = by train [preposition *mit* + masculine dative *dem Zug*], *nach München* = to Munich [preposition *nach* for cities/countries without articles]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I travel by train to Munich." Describing travel means and destination.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *fahren* (position 2) + dative preposition *mit* + masculine dative contraction/article (*dem Zug*) + directional preposition (*nach* + city name).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Preposition rule: ALL vehicles take *mit + Dativ* (*mit dem Zug*, *mit dem Bus*, *mit dem Auto*, *mit der U-Bahn*, *mit dem Fahrrad*).

**#152. Eine Fahrkarte, bitte.** — A ticket, please.
*Example sentence:* Eine Fahrkarte nach Hamburg, bitte. (A ticket to Hamburg, please.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Eine Fahrkarte nach Hamburg, bitte* = A ticket to Hamburg, please (*eine Fahrkarte* = a travel ticket [feminine accusative indefinite article], *nach Hamburg* = to Hamburg [directional preposition], *bitte* = please).
  - *Full meaning:* "A ticket to Hamburg, please." Buying a train ticket at a ticket counter.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Elliptical polite request: feminine accusative object (*eine Fahrkarte*) + directional destination (*nach Hamburg*) + politeness particle (*bitte*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Classic prompt in *Sprechen Teil 3* when receiving an image card showing a train ticket or ticket machine.

**#153. Wann kommt der nächste Bus?** — When does the next bus come?
*Example sentence:* Wann kommt der nächste Bus? Ich warte schon zehn Minuten. (When does the next bus come? I've been waiting for ten minutes.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Wann kommt der nächste Bus?* = When does the next bus come? (*wann* = when [question word for time], *kommt* = comes, *der nächste Bus* = the next bus [masculine nominative with definite adjective ending *-e*]).
  - *Ich warte schon zehn Minuten* = I have already been waiting for ten minutes (*ich warte* = I wait, *schon* = already, *zehn Minuten* = ten minutes [time duration]).
  - *Full meaning:* "When does the next bus come? I've been waiting for ten minutes." Waiting at a bus stop.
- **Grammar & Structure:** W-question: question word of time *Wann* (position 1) + verb *kommt* (position 2) + subject in nominative with adjective ending *-e* (*der nächste Bus*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Tested in *Hören Teil 1* train/bus station audio announcements.

**#154. Ich nehme die U-Bahn.** — I take the subway.
*Example sentence:* Ich nehme die U-Bahn zur Arbeit, das geht schneller. (I take the subway to work, it's faster.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich nehme die U-Bahn zur Arbeit* = I take the subway to work (*ich nehme* = I take [from irregular verb *nehmen*], *die U-Bahn* = the subway/underground [feminine accusative], *zur Arbeit* = to work [*zu + der Arbeit*, feminine dative contraction]).
  - *das geht schneller* = that goes faster / that's quicker (*das geht* = that goes/works, *schneller* = faster [comparative of *schnell*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I take the subway to work, it's faster." Explaining transport preference.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + irregular verb *nehmen* (position 2, *nehme*; 2nd/3rd person: *du nimmst / er nimmt*) + feminine direct object in accusative (*die U-Bahn*) + dative direction contraction (*zu + der = zur Arbeit*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Irregular verb conjugation: *nehmen* changes stem vowel from *e* to *i/imm* in 2nd and 3rd person singular.

**#155. Der Zug hat Verspätung.** — The train is delayed.
*Example sentence:* Der Zug hat Verspätung, er kommt zwanzig Minuten später. (The train is delayed, it arrives twenty minutes later.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Der Zug hat Verspätung* = The train is delayed (Lit. "The train has delay": *der Zug* = the train, *hat* = has, *Verspätung* = delay [feminine noun used without article]).
  - *er kommt zwanzig Minuten später* = it arrives twenty minutes later (*er* = it/he [pronoun referring to masculine *der Zug*], *kommt* = comes/arrives, *zwanzig Minuten später* = twenty minutes later).
  - *Full meaning:* "The train is delayed, it arrives twenty minutes later." Understanding train station announcements.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*der Zug*, masculine nominative) + verb *haben* (*hat*, position 2) + feminine noun without article (*Verspätung*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Vital audio announcement phrase in *Hören Teil 2 & 3* (*"Achtung an Gleis 3: Der ICE nach Frankfurt hat circa 15 Minuten Verspätung"*).

**#156. Wo ist die Haltestelle?** — Where is the (bus/tram) stop?
*Example sentence:* Wo ist die Haltestelle für den Bus Nummer fünf? (Where is the stop for bus number five?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Wo ist die Haltestelle für den Bus Nummer fünf?* = Where is the stop for bus number five? (*wo ist* = where is, *die Haltestelle* = the bus/tram stop, *für den Bus* = for the bus [*für* takes accusative masculine *den Bus*], *Nummer fünf* = number five).
  - *Full meaning:* "Where is the stop for bus number five?" Asking directions at a transit hub.
- **Grammar & Structure:** W-question: *Wo* (position 1) + copula *ist* (position 2) + feminine subject in nominative (*die Haltestelle*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Key direction inquiry for *Sprechen Teil 2 & 3*. Remember: *die Haltestelle* is for bus/tram, whereas *der Bahnhof* is for trains.

**#157. Ich gehe zu Fuß.** — I walk / I go on foot.
*Example sentence:* Der Supermarkt ist nah, ich gehe zu Fuß. (The supermarket is close, I walk.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Der Supermarkt ist nah* = The supermarket is close/nearby (*der Supermarkt* = the supermarket, *ist* = is, *nah* = close/near).
  - *ich gehe zu Fuß* = I walk / I go on foot (*ich gehe* = I go, *zu Fuß* = on foot [fixed German prepositional idiom; never say *mit den Füßen*]).
  - *Full meaning:* "The supermarket is close, I walk." Choosing walking over motorized transport.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + motion verb *gehen* (position 2) + fixed prepositional idiom (*zu Fuß* = on foot).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Contrast with vehicle travel: do not say *mit den Füßen*; the fixed standard German idiom is always *zu Fuß gehen*.

**#158. Ich fahre mit dem Auto.** — I go by car.
*Example sentence:* Ich fahre mit dem Auto zur Arbeit, es dauert dreißig Minuten. (I go by car to work, it takes thirty minutes.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich fahre mit dem Auto zur Arbeit* = I drive to work by car (*ich fahre* = I drive/ride, *mit dem Auto* = by car [preposition *mit* + neuter dative *dem Auto*], *zur Arbeit* = to work [*zu + der*]).
  - *es dauert dreißig Minuten* = it takes thirty minutes (*es dauert* = it lasts/takes [from *dauern* for time duration], *dreißig Minuten* = thirty minutes).
  - *Full meaning:* "I go by car to work, it takes thirty minutes." Commute details and travel time.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *fahren* (position 2) + dative preposition *mit* + neuter dative article (*dem Auto*) + directional phrase (*zur Arbeit*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Expressing duration with *dauern*: *"es dauert [Zeit]"* is frequently asked in *Sprechen Teil 2* (*"Wie lange dauert die Fahrt?"*).

**#159. Muss ich umsteigen?** — Do I have to transfer / change trains?
*Example sentence:* Muss ich umsteigen oder fährt der Bus direkt? (Do I have to transfer or does the bus go directly?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Muss ich umsteigen* = Do I have to transfer / change trains? (*muss ich* = must I [modal *müssen* in question position 1], *umsteigen* = transfer/switch vehicles [separable verb infinitive at end]).
  - *oder fährt der Bus direkt?* = or does the bus go directly? (*oder* = or, *fährt der Bus* = goes the bus, *direkt* = directly).
  - *Full meaning:* "Do I have to transfer or does the bus go directly?" Clarifying route connections.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Yes/No question with modal verb *müssen* in position 1 (*muss*) + subject *ich* (position 2) + separable infinitive *umsteigen* at sentence end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential travel query at ticket counters or on trains (*Hören Teil 2* and *Sprechen Teil 2*).

**#160. Welche Linie fährt zum Bahnhof?** — Which line goes to the train station?
*Example sentence:* Welche Linie fährt zum Bahnhof? Die Linie drei. (Which line goes to the train station? Line three.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Welche Linie fährt zum Bahnhof?* = Which line goes to the train station? (*welche Linie* = which line [interrogative pronoun *welche* in feminine nominative], *fährt* = travels/runs, *zum Bahnhof* = to the train station [*zu + dem*, dative masculine]).
  - *Die Linie drei* = Line three (*die Linie* = the line, *drei* = three).
  - *Full meaning:* "Which line goes to the train station? Line three." Tram or bus route information.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Interrogative pronoun in feminine nominative (*welche Linie*) in position 1 + verb *fährt* (position 2) + directional contraction with dative (*zu + dem = zum Bahnhof*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Notice the interrogative pronoun *welcher/welche/welches* declining like a definite article.

**#161. Ich brauche ein Taxi.** — I need a taxi.
*Example sentence:* Es ist spät, ich brauche ein Taxi nach Hause. (It is late, I need a taxi home.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Es ist spät* = It is late (*es ist* = it is, *spät* = late).
  - *ich brauche ein Taxi nach Hause* = I need a taxi home (*ich brauche* = I need, *ein Taxi* = a taxi [neuter accusative object], *nach Hause* = [towards] home).
  - *Full meaning:* "It is late, I need a taxi home." Calling or hailing a taxi late at night.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *brauchen* (position 2) + neuter direct object in accusative (*ein Taxi*) + directional adverb (*nach Hause*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Remember the difference: *nach Hause* (homeward, motion towards home) vs. *zu Hause* (at home, stationary location).

---

## Phone and Communication

**#162. Kann ich telefonieren?** — Can I make a phone call?
*Example sentence:* Kann ich telefonieren? Ich muss meine Mutter anrufen. (Can I make a phone call? I need to call my mother.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Kann ich telefonieren?* = Can I make a phone call? (*kann ich* = can I [modal request with *können*], *telefonieren* = make a phone call [intransitive activity of speaking on phone]).
  - *Ich muss meine Mutter anrufen* = I need to call my mother (*ich muss* = I must [modal *müssen*], *meine Mutter* = my mother [feminine accusative direct object], *anrufen* = to call someone on phone [transitive separable verb]).
  - *Full meaning:* "Can I make a phone call? I need to call my mother." Asking permission to make an urgent call.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Yes/No request with modal verb *können* in position 1 (*kann*) + subject *ich* (position 2) + main infinitive at end (*telefonieren*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High-utility polite request in *Sprechen Teil 3* when receiving a card with a telephone icon.

**#163. Meine Nummer ist…** — My number is…
*Example sentence:* Meine Nummer ist null-eins-sieben-zwei-drei-vier-fünf. (My number is 0172345.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Meine Nummer ist null-eins-sieben-zwei-drei-vier-fünf* = My number is 0172345 (*meine Nummer* = my telephone number [feminine subject], *ist* = is, *null-eins-sieben-zwei-drei-vier-fünf* = zero-one-seven-two-three-four-five [digits dictated individually]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My number is 0172345." Giving personal contact information in exam interviews.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*meine Nummer*, feminine nominative) + copula *ist* (position 2) + digit sequence.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Mandatory testing point in *Sprechen Teil 1* and *Hören Teil 1*: candidates must say their telephone number digit by digit and correctly transcribe phone numbers heard in audio clips.

**#164. Ich schreibe eine E-Mail.** — I am writing an email.
*Example sentence:* Ich schreibe eine E-Mail an meinen Lehrer. (I am writing an email to my teacher.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich schreibe eine E-Mail an meinen Lehrer* = I am writing an email to my teacher (*ich schreibe* = I write, *eine E-Mail* = an email [feminine accusative], *an meinen Lehrer* = to my teacher [preposition *an* takes accusative masculine *meinen Lehrer* when indicating recipient]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am writing an email to my teacher." Stating communication activities in language courses.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + verb *schreiben* (position 2) + feminine direct object in accusative (*eine E-Mail*) + preposition *an* + masculine accusative noun (*meinen Lehrer*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Core context for *Schreiben Teil 2*, where test-takers are required to write a short formal or informal email of approximately 30 words.

**#165. Kann ich eine Nachricht hinterlassen?** — Can I leave a message?
*Example sentence:* Er ist nicht da. Kann ich eine Nachricht hinterlassen? (He is not here. Can I leave a message?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Er ist nicht da* = He is not here / not present (*er* = he, *ist* = is, *nicht da* = not here/there).
  - *Kann ich eine Nachricht hinterlassen?* = Can I leave a message? (*kann ich* = can I [modal question], *eine Nachricht* = a message [feminine accusative], *hinterlassen* = leave behind [inseparable verb infinitive]).
  - *Full meaning:* "He is not here. Can I leave a message?" Professional telephone protocol.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal verb *können* (position 1, *kann*) + subject *ich* + feminine direct object in accusative (*eine Nachricht*) + inseparable infinitive at end (*hinterlassen*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Professional telephone phrase heard in *Hören Teil 1* voice mail and office dialogues.

**#166. Ich rufe dich später an.** — I'll call you later.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin jetzt beschäftigt, ich rufe dich später an. (I am busy now, I'll call you later.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich bin jetzt beschäftigt* = I am busy right now (*ich bin* = I am, *jetzt* = now, *beschäftigt* = busy/occupied).
  - *ich rufe dich später an* = I'll call you later (*ich* = I, *rufe ... an* = call [separable verb *anrufen*], *dich* = you [informal accusative personal pronoun], *später* = later).
  - *Full meaning:* "I am busy now, I'll call you later." Postponing a phone conversation politely.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + separable verb *anrufen* (conjugated stem *rufe* in position 2, prefix *an* at the end) + informal personal pronoun in accusative (*dich*) + temporal adverb (*später*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Contrast *anrufen* (transitive, takes accusative object: *Ich rufe dich an*) with *telefonieren* (intransitive: *Ich telefoniere mit dir*). Formal version: *"Ich rufe Sie später an."*

**#167. Hast du meine SMS bekommen?** — Did you get my text message?
*Example sentence:* Hast du meine SMS bekommen? Ich habe dir geschrieben. (Did you get my text message? I wrote to you.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Hast du meine SMS bekommen?* = Did you get my text message? (*hast du ... bekommen* = have you received [Perfekt of inseparable verb *bekommen*], *meine SMS* = my SMS/text message [feminine accusative]).
  - *Ich habe dir geschrieben* = I wrote to you (*ich habe ... geschrieben* = I have written [Perfekt of *schreiben*], *dir* = to you [informal dative personal pronoun]).
  - *Full meaning:* "Did you get my text message? I wrote to you." Checking whether a mobile message was received.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Perfekt tense question: auxiliary verb *haben* (position 1, *hast*) + informal subject *du* (position 2) + feminine accusative object (*meine SMS*) + Partizip II at end (*bekommen*, inseparable verb with prefix *be-*, no *ge-*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Inseparable verbs starting with *be-*, *ge-*, *er-*, *ver-*, *zer-* do NOT take *ge-* in the Partizip II (*bekommen* -> *bekommen*).

**#168. Wie ist Ihre E-Mail-Adresse?** — What is your email address?
*Example sentence:* Wie ist Ihre E-Mail-Adresse? Ich schicke Ihnen die Informationen. (What is your email address? I will send you the information.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Wie ist Ihre E-Mail-Adresse?* = What is your email address? (Lit. "How is your email address?": *wie* = how [standard German question word for address, phone number, name], *ist* = is, *Ihre E-Mail-Adresse* = your email address [formal polite possessive *Ihre*, capitalized]).
  - *Ich schicke Ihnen die Informationen* = I will send you the information (*ich schicke* = I send, *Ihnen* = to you [formal dative pronoun], *die Informationen* = the information [accusative plural]).
  - *Full meaning:* "What is your email address? I will send you the information." Exchanging contact details professionally.
- **Grammar & Structure:** W-question: question word *Wie* (position 1) + copula *ist* (position 2) + formal possessive article with feminine nominative ending (*Ihre E-Mail-Adresse*, capitalized).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** In German, questions asking for phone numbers, addresses, and names use *Wie* ("How is..."), NOT *Was* (*"Was ist Ihre Adresse"* is an English calque). Tested in *Sprechen Teil 1 & 2*.

**#169. Mein Handy ist kaputt.** — My mobile phone is broken.
*Example sentence:* Mein Handy ist kaputt, ich kann nicht anrufen. (My mobile phone is broken, I cannot call.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Mein Handy ist kaputt* = My mobile phone is broken (*mein Handy* = my cell phone [neuter nominative], *ist* = is, *kaputt* = out of order / broken [colloquial standard predicate adjective]).
  - *ich kann nicht anrufen* = I cannot call (*ich kann* = I can [modal *können*], *nicht* = not, *anrufen* = make a phone call [infinitive]).
  - *Full meaning:* "My mobile phone is broken, I cannot call." Explaining communication failure.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*mein Handy*, neuter nominative) + copula *ist* (position 2) + predicate adjective (*kaputt*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Frequent problem scenario in *Schreiben Teil 2* apology notes explaining delayed communication or requesting another way to connect.

**#170. Ich schicke dir eine Nachricht.** — I will send you a message.
*Example sentence:* Ich schicke dir eine Nachricht, wenn ich ankomme. (I will send you a message when I arrive.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  - *Ich schicke dir eine Nachricht* = I will send you a message (*ich schicke* = I send, *dir* = to you [informal dative pronoun], *eine Nachricht* = a message [feminine accusative object]).
  - *wenn ich ankomme* = when I arrive (*wenn* = when/if [subordinating conjunction], *ich* = I, *ankomme* = arrive [separable verb conjugated with prefix attached at clause end]).
  - *Full meaning:* "I'll send you a message when I arrive." Reassuring friends/family during transit.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject (*ich*) + dual-object verb *schicken* (position 2) + indirect object pronoun in dative (*dir* / formal: *Ihnen*) + direct object in feminine accusative (*eine Nachricht*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Dative pronoun precedes accusative noun: in German, pronoun objects precede noun objects (*Ich schicke dir eine Nachricht*).

---

This section contains 80 essential German sayings and everyday expressions (#171–#250) for the Goethe-Zertifikat A1 exam. Each entry includes the core phrase, English translation, contextual example sentence, detailed chunk-by-chunk sentence breakdown with literal/idiomatic meanings, grammatical breakdown (verb position, case, declension), and targeted exam/usage tips.

---

## Housing and Living (#171–#182)

**#171. Ich wohne in einer Wohnung.** — I live in an apartment.
*Example sentence:* Ich wohne in einer Wohnung in Berlin. (I live in an apartment in Berlin.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich wohne* = "I live / reside" (subject pronoun *ich* + 1st person singular of *wohnen*).
  • *in einer Wohnung* = "in an apartment / flat" (*in* + feminine dative *einer Wohnung*, specifying the type of home).
  • *in Berlin* = "in Berlin" (preposition *in* + city name without article).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I reside in an apartment in Berlin." A standard, natural way to state both your housing type and city of residence.
