- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Ich") + conjugated regular verb ("wohne", 1st person singular of *wohnen*, V2 position) + prepositional phrase with two-way preposition "in" governing the Dative case for stationary location ("einer Wohnung", feminine dative indefinite article "einer").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential formula for Goethe A1 Sprechen Teil 1 (Sich vorstellen: Wohnort) and Schreiben Teil 2 (describing where you live or inviting someone over).

**#172. Die Wohnung hat drei Zimmer.** — The apartment has three rooms.
*Example sentence:* Die Wohnung hat drei Zimmer, eine Küche und ein Bad. (The apartment has three rooms, a kitchen, and a bathroom.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Die Wohnung* = "The apartment / flat" (definite feminine article + noun).
  • *hat* = "has" (3rd person singular of *haben*).
  • *drei Zimmer* = "three rooms" (cardinal number *drei* + plural neuter noun *Zimmer*). Note that in German, *Zimmer* counts living rooms and bedrooms, while kitchens and bathrooms are listed separately.
  • *eine Küche* = "a kitchen" (feminine accusative indefinite article *eine* + noun).
  • *und ein Bad* = "and a bathroom" (coordinating conjunction *und* + neuter accusative article *ein* + noun *Bad*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "The apartment has three rooms, a kitchen, and a bath."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject in Nominative ("Die Wohnung", feminine singular) + irregular verb *haben* in 3rd person singular ("hat", V2) + direct object in Accusative ("drei Zimmer", neuter plural noun with cardinal number zero article).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High frequency in Lesen Teil 2/3 (apartment advertisements / Wohnungsanzeigen) and Sprechen Teil 2 with prompt card "Wohnung" or "Zimmer".

**#173. Die Miete kostet 500 Euro im Monat.** — The rent costs 500 euros a month.
*Example sentence:* Die Miete kostet 500 Euro im Monat, das ist nicht teuer. (The rent costs 500 euros a month; that's not expensive.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Die Miete* = "The rent" (feminine subject noun).
  • *kostet 500 Euro* = "costs 500 euros" (verb *kosten* + currency amount in Accusative).
  • *im Monat* = "per month / in a month" (contracted *in dem Monat*).
  • *das ist* = "that is" (demonstrative subject *das* + copula *ist*).
  • *nicht teuer* = "not expensive" (negation particle *nicht* + adjective *teuer*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "The rent costs 500 Euro in-the month, that is not expensive." Used to describe affordable living expenses.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Die Miete", feminine) + verb *kosten* ("kostet", 3rd person singular, V2) + price measurement in Accusative ("500 Euro") + temporal prepositional phrase ("im Monat" = contracted *in dem Monat*, masculine dative).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High frequency in Hören Teil 2 and Lesen Teil 1/2 when comparing housing costs, utility bills, and rental agreements.

**#174. Ich suche eine Wohnung.** — I'm looking for an apartment.
*Example sentence:* Ich suche eine Wohnung in der Stadtmitte. (I'm looking for an apartment in the city center.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich suche* = "I am looking for / I search" (German simple present translates both English simple present and present continuous).
  • *eine Wohnung* = "an apartment" (direct object in feminine Accusative: *eine* + *Wohnung*).
  • *in der Stadtmitte* = "in the city center / downtown" (preposition *in* + feminine dative *der Stadtmitte* indicating stationary location).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I seek an apartment in the city-center."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject pronoun ("Ich") + regular transitive verb *suchen* ("suche", 1st person singular, V2) + direct object in feminine Accusative ("eine Wohnung", feminine accusative indefinite article "eine").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Ideal opening line in Schreiben Teil 2 (writing an inquiry email to a landlord, flatmate group, or housing agency).

**#175. Das Zimmer ist sehr groß.** — The room is very big.
*Example sentence:* Das Zimmer ist sehr groß und hat viel Licht. (The room is very big and has a lot of light.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Das Zimmer* = "The room" (neuter subject noun).
  • *ist sehr groß* = "is very large / big" (copula *ist* + degree adverb *sehr* + predicate adjective *groß*).
  • *und hat* = "and has" (conjunction + verb).
  • *viel Licht* = "a lot of light / plenty of daylight" (uninflected quantifier *viel* + neuter noun *Licht*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "The room is very big and has much light." Standard positive descriptor for living spaces.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Das Zimmer", neuter nominative) + copular verb *sein* ("ist", 3rd person singular, V2) + intensifying degree adverb ("sehr") + predicate adjective ("groß", uninflected in predicative position).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Frequently tested in Sprechen Teil 2 (describing rooms or accommodation) and Hören Teil 1/2 when listening to housing descriptions.

**#176. Die Küche ist klein, aber schön.** — The kitchen is small but nice.
*Example sentence:* Die Küche ist klein, aber schön und modern. (The kitchen is small but nice and modern.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Die Küche* = "The kitchen" (feminine subject noun).
  • *ist klein* = "is small" (verb *ist* + adjective *klein*).
  • *aber* = "but" (adversative coordinating conjunction introducing a contrast without shifting word order).
  • *schön und modern* = "beautiful/nice and modern" (two coordinated descriptive adjectives).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "The kitchen is small, but beautiful and modern." Shows how to balance a drawback with positive features.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Die Küche", feminine) + copula *sein* ("ist", V2) + compound predicate adjectives joined by the adversative coordinating conjunction "aber" (position 0, does not affect word order).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Great for A1 writing tasks to connect two contrasting descriptive adjectives using "aber" without changing standard SVO order.

**#177. Ich wohne im zweiten Stock.** — I live on the second floor.
*Example sentence:* Ich wohne im zweiten Stock, es gibt keinen Aufzug. (I live on the second floor; there is no elevator.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich wohne* = "I live / reside".
  • *im zweiten Stock* = "on the second floor" (contracted *in dem* + ordinal number *zweiten* with dative ending *-en* + masculine noun *Stock*). Note: in German-speaking countries, "2. Stock" is two levels above the ground floor (equivalent to the 3rd floor in the US).
  • *es gibt* = "there is / there exists" (idiomatic fixed phrase strictly demanding Accusative).
  • *keinen Aufzug* = "no elevator" (negative article in masculine Accusative *keinen* + noun *Aufzug*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I live in-the second floor, it gives no elevator."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Ich") + verb *wohnen* ("wohne", V2) + contracted preposition "im" (*in dem*) + ordinal number with masculine Dative adjective ending ("zweiten") + masculine noun ("Stock"). Also illustrates impersonal "es gibt" + masculine accusative ("keinen Aufzug").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Crucial for Hören Teil 1 (identifying addresses, apartment levels, and floor numbers) and Sprechen Teil 1 when stating exact address details.

**#178. Wir haben einen Balkon.** — We have a balcony.
*Example sentence:* Wir haben einen Balkon mit Blick auf den Garten. (We have a balcony with a view of the garden.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wir haben* = "We have" (plural subject pronoun + verb *haben*).
  • *einen Balkon* = "a balcony" (masculine direct object in Accusative: *der Balkon* -> *einen Balkon*).
  • *mit Blick auf* = "with a view of / overlooking" (dative preposition *mit* + noun *Blick* + directional preposition *auf*).
  • *den Garten* = "the garden" (masculine accusative *den Garten* governed by *auf*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "We have a balcony with view onto the garden."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Plural subject pronoun ("Wir") + verb *haben* ("haben", 1st person plural, V2) + direct object in masculine Accusative ("einen Balkon", *der Balkon* -> *einen Balkon*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** A classic Goethe A1 test of the masculine accusative ending "-en" (*einen*) vs nominative (*ein*). Frequently appears on Sprechen Teil 2 cards with the prompt word "Balkon".

**#179. Ich brauche neue Möbel.** — I need new furniture.
*Example sentence:* Ich brauche neue Möbel für mein Schlafzimmer. (I need new furniture for my bedroom.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich brauche* = "I need".
  • *neue Möbel* = "new furniture" (adjective *neue* + plural noun *Möbel*; note that *Möbel* in German is plural, unlike English uncountable furniture).
  • *für* = "for" (preposition strictly demanding Accusative).
  • *mein Schlafzimmer* = "my bedroom" (possessive determiner *mein* + compound neuter noun *Schlafzimmer* in Accusative).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I need new furnitures for my sleeping-room."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Transitive verb *brauchen* ("brauche", V2) + direct object plural noun (*die Möbel*) preceded by an adjective with zero-article plural accusative ending ("neue Möbel") + prepositional phrase with *für* (+ accusative neuter "mein Schlafzimmer").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common in Schreiben Teil 2 (writing a note to a friend asking for help moving, shopping for furniture, or visiting IKEA).

**#180. Die Heizung funktioniert nicht.** — The heating doesn't work.
*Example sentence:* Die Heizung funktioniert nicht, es ist sehr kalt. (The heating doesn't work; it's very cold.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Die Heizung* = "The heating / heater" (feminine subject noun).
  • *funktioniert nicht* = "does not function / does not work" (verb *funktionieren* + final negation particle *nicht*).
  • *es ist* = "it is" (impersonal weather/temperature subject *es* + copula *ist*).
  • *sehr kalt* = "very cold" (intensifier *sehr* + adjective *kalt*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "The heating functions not, it is very cold."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Die Heizung", feminine) + regular verb *funktionieren* ("funktioniert", 3rd person singular, V2) + sentence negation particle "nicht" positioned at the end of the clause to negate the verb.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High-yield template for Goethe A1 Schreiben Teil 2 (writing a formal complaint email to the landlord/Hausverwaltung reporting a broken appliance or heating failure).

**#181. Mein Nachbar ist sehr nett.** — My neighbor is very nice.
*Example sentence:* Mein Nachbar ist sehr nett und hilft mir oft. (My neighbor is very nice and often helps me.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Mein Nachbar* = "My neighbor" (masculine singular subject noun phrase).
  • *ist sehr nett* = "is very kind / friendly" (copula *ist* + adverb *sehr* + predicate adjective *nett*).
  • *und hilft* = "and helps" (coordinating conjunction *und* + 3rd person singular stem-changing verb *helfen*, *e* -> *i*).
  • *mir* = "me" (1st person personal pronoun in Dative, required by *helfen*).
  • *oft* = "often / frequently" (temporal frequency adverb).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "My neighbor is very nice and helps to-me often."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Possessive determiner + noun ("Mein Nachbar", masculine nominative) + copula verb "ist" (V2) + predicate adjective. The second clause demonstrates dative object government with the verb *helfen* ("hilft mir", *mir* = 1st person dative pronoun).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Useful for Sprechen Teil 2 (topic "Nachbarn" / neighbors) and demonstrating correct dative pronoun usage with *helfen*.

**#182. Ich wohne bei meinen Eltern.** — I live with my parents.
*Example sentence:* Ich wohne bei meinen Eltern, aber ich suche bald eine eigene Wohnung. (I live with my parents, but I'm looking for my own apartment soon.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich wohne* = "I live / reside".
  • *bei meinen Eltern* = "with my parents" (dative preposition *bei* meaning "at the home of" + plural dative possessive *meinen* + noun *Eltern* with plural dative *-n*).
  • *aber* = "but" (adversative conjunction).
  • *ich suche* = "I am looking for / I search".
  • *bald* = "soon" (temporal adverb).
  • *eine eigene Wohnung* = "an apartment of my own / an own flat" (feminine accusative phrase: *eine* + inflected adjective *eigene* + noun *Wohnung*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I live at my parents, but I seek soon an own apartment."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Ich") + verb *wohnen* ("wohne", V2) + dative preposition "bei" strictly governing the plural dative possessive article and noun ("meinen Eltern", with dative plural noun suffix "-n").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard response in Sprechen Teil 1 (personal details / living situation). Always remember that the preposition "bei" demands the Dative case.

---

## Appointments and Plans (#183–#193)

**#183. Ich habe einen Termin beim Arzt.** — I have an appointment at the doctor's.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe einen Termin beim Arzt um 10 Uhr. (I have an appointment at the doctor's at 10 o'clock.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich habe* = "I have" (subject + verb *haben*).
  • *einen Termin* = "an appointment" (masculine direct object in Accusative: *der Termin* -> *einen Termin*).
  • *beim Arzt* = "at the doctor's (office)" (contracted dative phrase *bei dem Arzt*).
  • *um 10 Uhr* = "at 10 o'clock" (preposition *um* for exact clock time + hour).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I have an appointment at-the doctor at 10 o'clock."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Ich") + verb *haben* ("habe", V2) + masculine accusative direct object ("einen Termin") + contracted dative preposition "beim" (*bei dem*) + masculine noun *Arzt* + temporal preposition "um" + clock time.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Extremely frequent in Sprechen Teil 3 (arranging appointments / making plans) and Schreiben Teil 2 (excusing an absence from class or work due to a medical visit).

**#184. Wann treffen wir uns?** — When are we meeting?
*Example sentence:* Wann treffen wir uns morgen? (When are we meeting tomorrow?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wann* = "When" (W-question word asking about time).
  • *treffen* = "meet" (conjugated verb in V2 position).
  • *wir* = "we" (subject pronoun inverted to position 3).
  • *uns* = "each other / ourselves" (reciprocal reflexive pronoun in Accusative).
  • *morgen* = "tomorrow" (temporal adverb, lowercase).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "When meet we ourselves tomorrow?" The standard German formula for planning a rendezvous.
- **Grammar & Structure:** Interrogative temporal adverb "Wann" (W-word, position 1) + conjugated reciprocal verb *treffen* ("treffen", 1st person plural, V2) + subject ("wir", position 3) + reflexive pronoun in accusative ("uns").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Fundamental question for Goethe A1 Sprechen Teil 3 (negotiating a mutually convenient meeting time or activity with your partner).

**#185. Morgen gehe ich ins Kino.** — Tomorrow I'm going to the cinema.
*Example sentence:* Morgen gehe ich ins Kino mit meiner Freundin. (Tomorrow I'm going to the cinema with my girlfriend.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Morgen* = "Tomorrow" (temporal adverb in position 1, causing inversion).
  • *gehe ich* = "go I" (conjugated verb *gehe* in V2 + subject *ich* in position 3).
  • *ins Kino* = "to the cinema / movies" (contracted *in das Kino*, neuter accusative showing movement into a place).
  • *mit meiner Freundin* = "with my female friend / girlfriend" (dative preposition *mit* + feminine dative possessive *meiner* + noun *Freundin*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Tomorrow go I into-the cinema with my girlfriend."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Temporal adverbial "Morgen" at position 1 triggers subject-verb inversion: conjugated verb "gehe" stays at V2, subject pronoun "ich" moves to position 3 + directional preposition contraction "ins" (*in das*, neuter accusative).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Demonstrates the fundamental V2 inversion rule in German. German regularly uses present tense + future time adverbial (*morgen*) to express scheduled future actions.

**#186. Können wir den Termin verschieben?** — Can we reschedule the appointment?
*Example sentence:* Können wir den Termin auf Freitag verschieben? (Can we reschedule the appointment to Friday?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Können wir* = "Can we" (modal verb *können* in position 1 for yes/no question + subject *wir*).
  • *den Termin* = "the appointment" (masculine direct object in Accusative: *den Termin*).
  • *auf Freitag* = "to Friday" (preposition *auf* + day of the week, indicating the target rescheduled date).
  • *verschieben* = "postpone / shift / reschedule" (infinitive verb placed at the very end to complete the modal sentence bracket).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Can we the appointment onto Friday shift?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal verb *können* at position 1 (yes/no question structure) + subject ("wir") + masculine accusative direct object ("den Termin") + prepositional phrase ("auf Freitag") + main infinitive verb at the end of the clause ("verschieben", modal sentence bracket / Satzklammer).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential formula for Schreiben Teil 2 when politely requesting to postpone an exam, a language course, or an appointment.

**#187. Ich komme um drei Uhr.** — I'll come at three o'clock.
*Example sentence:* Ich komme um drei Uhr, ist das okay? (I'll come at three o'clock; is that okay?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich komme* = "I am coming / I will come" (subject + verb *kommen*).
  • *um drei Uhr* = "at three o'clock" (preposition *um* for exact time + number + *Uhr*).
  • *ist das okay?* = "is that okay / alright?" (verb *ist* + demonstrative *das* + loanword adjective *okay*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I come at three clock, is that okay?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Ich") + regular verb *kommen* ("komme", V2) + temporal preposition "um" (strictly governing exact clock times) + cardinal numeral ("drei Uhr").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** The preposition "um" is exclusively used for clock times in German. Tested across Hören Teil 1 and required during Sprechen Teil 3 scheduling.

**#188. Am Wochenende fahre ich nach Hamburg.** — On the weekend I'm going to Hamburg.
*Example sentence:* Am Wochenende fahre ich nach Hamburg, meine Schwester wohnt dort. (On the weekend I'm going to Hamburg; my sister lives there.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Am Wochenende* = "On the weekend" (contracted *an dem Wochenende*, neuter dative temporal phrase).
  • *fahre ich* = "travel I / drive I" (inversion: verb *fahre* at V2 + subject *ich* at position 3).
  • *nach Hamburg* = "to Hamburg" (preposition *nach* used for travel destinations to cities and countries without grammatical articles).
  • *meine Schwester* = "my sister" (feminine subject).
  • *wohnt dort* = "lives there" (verb *wohnen* + local adverb *dort*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "At-the weekend travel I to Hamburg, my sister lives there."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Temporal prepositional phrase "Am Wochenende" (*an dem Wochenende*, neuter dative) in position 1 causes inversion -> conjugated verb "fahre" at V2 -> subject "ich" at position 3 + geographical destination preposition "nach" (used for cities and countries without grammatical articles).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Key phrase for discussing weekend leisure plans in Sprechen Teil 2 and writing informal postcards or messages in Schreiben Teil 2.

**#189. Hast du am Samstag Zeit?** — Do you have time on Saturday?
*Example sentence:* Hast du am Samstag Zeit? Wir können zusammen essen. (Do you have time on Saturday? We can eat together.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Hast du* = "Have you / Do you have" (verb *haben* in position 1 + informal subject *du*).
  • *am Samstag* = "on Saturday" (contracted *an dem Samstag*, masculine dative day of the week).
  • *Zeit* = "time" (uncountable direct object in Accusative).
  • *Wir können* = "We can" (modal verb *können*).
  • *zusammen essen* = "eat together" (manner adverb *zusammen* + infinitive *essen* at sentence end).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Have you on-the Saturday time? We can together eat."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Verb-initial yes/no question: conjugated verb *haben* ("Hast", 2nd person singular informal, position 1) + subject pronoun ("du") + temporal contraction "am" (*an dem*, masculine dative used for days of the week) + direct object noun "Zeit" in Accusative (uncountable, zero article).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard informal conversation starter for peer dialogue in Sprechen Teil 3 (suggesting an outing, dinner, or joint activity).

**#190. Ich bin leider schon verabredet.** — I already have plans, unfortunately.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin leider schon verabredet, vielleicht nächste Woche? (I already have plans, unfortunately; maybe next week?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich bin* = "I am".
  • *leider* = "unfortunately / regrettably" (adverb expressing regret).
  • *schon* = "already" (temporal adverb).
  • *verabredet* = "having plans / appointed" (participial predicate adjective from *sich verabreden*).
  • *vielleicht* = "maybe / perhaps" (adverb).
  • *nächste Woche* = "next week" (time accusative without preposition: feminine adjective *nächste* + noun *Woche*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I am unfortunately already appointed, maybe next week?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Ich") + copula *sein* ("bin", V2) + adverb of regret ("leider") + temporal adverb ("schon") + participial predicate adjective ("verabredet").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Polite and natural way to decline an invitation in Sprechen Teil 3 before proposing an alternative date, showing good pragmatic conversational ability.

**#191. Wir treffen uns am Bahnhof.** — We'll meet at the train station.
*Example sentence:* Wir treffen uns am Bahnhof um halb fünf. (We'll meet at the train station at half past four.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wir treffen uns* = "We meet (each other)" (reciprocal reflexive construction).
  • *am Bahnhof* = "at the train station" (contracted *an dem Bahnhof*, masculine dative for stationary location).
  • *um halb fünf* = "at 4:30" (preposition *um* + *halb fünf*, meaning half an hour before five = 4:30).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "We meet ourselves at-the train station at half five (4:30)."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Wir") + reciprocal verb *treffen* ("treffen", V2) + reflexive pronoun ("uns", accusative) + static locational preposition contraction "am" (*an dem*, masculine dative for stationary location).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High-frequency dialogue in Sprechen Teil 3 when agreeing on meeting locations. Contrast static location ("am Bahnhof", Dative) with directional movement ("zum Bahnhof", Dative).

**#192. Ich muss den Termin absagen.** — I have to cancel the appointment.
*Example sentence:* Ich muss den Termin absagen, ich bin krank. (I have to cancel the appointment; I'm sick.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich muss* = "I must / I have to" (modal verb *müssen*).
  • *den Termin* = "the appointment" (masculine direct object in Accusative: *den Termin*).
  • *absagen* = "cancel / call off" (separable prefix verb in infinitive at end of clause forming modal bracket).
  • *ich bin krank* = "I am sick / ill" (subject + copula *bin* + predicate adjective *krank*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I must the appointment cancel, I am sick."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal verb *müssen* ("muss", 1st person singular, V2) + masculine direct object in Accusative ("den Termin") + separable prefix verb in infinitive ("absagen") positioned at the final bracket position.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Mandatory structure for Schreiben Teil 2 formal letters (cancelling appointments, doctor visits, or language classes due to illness or professional obligations).

**#193. Was machen wir heute Abend?** — What are we doing this evening?
*Example sentence:* Was machen wir heute Abend? Gehen wir essen? (What are we doing this evening? Shall we go eat?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Was* = "What" (interrogative pronoun in position 1).
  • *machen wir* = "do we / are we doing" (verb in V2 + subject pronoun in position 3).
  • *heute Abend* = "this evening / tonight" (temporal adverbial phrase).
  • *Gehen wir essen?* = "Shall we go eat?" (verb-first suggestion with verb of movement *gehen* + bare infinitive *essen*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "What do we today evening? Go we eat?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Interrogative pronoun "Was" (position 1) + transitive verb *machen* ("machen", 1st person plural, V2) + subject pronoun "wir" (inversion, position 3) + temporal adverbial phrase "heute Abend".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common conversational starter in Sprechen Teil 3 when initiating free-time planning with an examination partner.

---

## Emergencies and Help (#194–#204)

**#194. Hilfe!** — Help!
*Example sentence:* Hilfe! Ich brauche einen Arzt! (Help! I need a doctor!)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Hilfe!* = "Help!" (urgent exclamation derived from the feminine noun *die Hilfe*).
  • *Ich brauche* = "I need" (subject + verb *brauchen*).
  • *einen Arzt!* = "a doctor!" (masculine accusative indefinite article *einen* + noun *Arzt*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Help! I need a doctor!"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Standalone exclamatory noun/interjection derived from the feminine noun *die Hilfe*. Requires an exclamation mark in written text.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Universal emergency cry recognized throughout all German-speaking countries. Vital listening comprehension item for urgent scenarios.

**#195. Rufen Sie die Polizei!** — Call the police!
*Example sentence:* Rufen Sie die Polizei! Jemand hat mein Auto gestohlen. (Call the police! Someone stole my car.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Rufen Sie* = "Call!" (formal imperative command addressed to an adult stranger).
  • *die Polizei!* = "the police!" (feminine direct object in Accusative).
  • *Jemand* = "Someone / somebody" (indefinite pronoun subject).
  • *hat ... gestohlen* = "has stolen" (Perfekt tense of irregular verb *stehlen*: auxiliary *hat* + past participle *gestohlen*).
  • *mein Auto* = "my car" (neuter direct object in Accusative).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Call you the police! Someone has my car stolen."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Formal imperative construction: infinitive verb *rufen* at position 1 + formal address pronoun "Sie" + direct object in feminine Accusative ("die Polizei").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Notice the formal imperative word order (Infinitive Verb + *Sie*). Critical for Sprechen Teil 3 (prompt card with "Notfall" or "Polizei") to give an urgent instruction.

**#196. Ich habe mein Handy verloren.** — I lost my cell phone.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe mein Handy verloren, können Sie mir helfen? (I lost my cell phone; can you help me?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich habe ... verloren* = "I have lost" (Perfekt of *verlieren*: auxiliary *habe* + Partizip II *verloren* at sentence end).
  • *mein Handy* = "my mobile phone / cell phone" (neuter direct object in Accusative).
  • *können Sie* = "can you" (formal polite modal request).
  • *mir helfen?* = "help me?" (dative pronoun *mir* governed by infinitive *helfen*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I have my cell phone lost, can you to-me help?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Compound past tense (Perfekt): auxiliary verb *haben* ("habe", V2) + neuter accusative object ("mein Handy") + past participle (Partizip II) of irregular verb *verlieren* ("verloren") at the sentence end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Very frequent in Sprechen Teil 2/3 (lost-and-found desk / Fundbüro scenario) and Schreiben Teil 2 when reporting lost property.

**#197. Rufen Sie einen Krankenwagen!** — Call an ambulance!
*Example sentence:* Rufen Sie einen Krankenwagen! Der Mann ist verletzt. (Call an ambulance! The man is injured.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Rufen Sie* = "Call!" (formal imperative command).
  • *einen Krankenwagen!* = "an ambulance!" (masculine accusative indefinite article *einen* + compound noun *Krankenwagen*, lit. "sick-person vehicle").
  • *Der Mann* = "The man" (masculine nominative subject).
  • *ist verletzt* = "is injured / hurt" (copula *ist* + participial predicate adjective *verletzt*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Call you a sick-car! The man is injured."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Formal imperative: verb *rufen* at position 1 + pronoun "Sie" + masculine accusative direct object ("einen Krankenwagen", masculine indefinite article ending in "-en").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential emergency phrase in Sprechen Teil 3. Tests masculine accusative inflection with emergency medical vocabulary.

**#198. Ich brauche dringend Hilfe.** — I urgently need help.
*Example sentence:* Ich brauche dringend Hilfe, mein Kind ist krank. (I urgently need help; my child is sick.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich brauche* = "I need" (subject + verb *brauchen*).
  • *dringend* = "urgently / desperately" (manner adverb).
  • *Hilfe* = "help / assistance" (uncountable abstract noun in Accusative, zero article).
  • *mein Kind* = "my child" (neuter subject noun phrase).
  • *ist krank* = "is sick / ill" (copula *ist* + adjective *krank*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I need urgently help, my child is sick."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject ("Ich") + transitive verb *brauchen* ("brauche", V2) + manner adverb ("dringend") + uncountable abstract direct object in Accusative ("Hilfe", zero article).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Key phrase for expressing acute distress, applicable in oral emergency roleplays and urgent written communications.

**#199. Wo ist das nächste Krankenhaus?** — Where is the nearest hospital?
*Example sentence:* Wo ist das nächste Krankenhaus? Es ist ein Notfall. (Where is the nearest hospital? It's an emergency.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wo ist* = "Where is" (local W-word *wo* + copula *ist*).
  • *das nächste Krankenhaus?* = "the nearest / closest hospital?" (definite neuter article *das* + superlative adjective *nächste* + compound noun *Krankenhaus*, lit. "sick-house").
  • *Es ist* = "It is" (subject *es* + *ist*).
  • *ein Notfall* = "an emergency" (masculine predicate noun in Nominative: *ein* + *Notfall*, lit. "need-case").
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Where is the nearest sick-house? It is an emergency."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Interrogative local adverb "Wo" (position 1) + copula "ist" (V2) + subject with superlative adjective in neuter Nominative ("das nächste Krankenhaus").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Core emergency formula for asking directions during critical situations in Sprechen Teil 2 and Teil 3.

**#200. Ich habe meinen Pass verloren.** — I lost my passport.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe meinen Pass verloren und brauche Hilfe von der Botschaft. (I lost my passport and need help from the embassy.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich habe ... verloren* = "I have lost" (Perfekt tense of *verlieren*).
  • *meinen Pass* = "my passport" (masculine accusative possessive *meinen* + *Pass*).
  • *und brauche* = "and need" (conjunction + verb).
  • *Hilfe* = "help".
  • *von der Botschaft* = "from the embassy" (dative preposition *von* + feminine dative article *der* + noun *Botschaft*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I have my passport lost and need help from the embassy."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Perfekt tense (*haben* + *verloren*) with masculine singular noun in Accusative: possessive article takes the required masculine accusative ending "-en" ("meinen Pass").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Classic scenario at police stations or consulates. Tests masculine accusative inflection of possessive determiners (*meinen*).

**#201. Es gibt einen Unfall.** — There is an accident.
*Example sentence:* Es gibt einen Unfall auf der Straße, bitte kommen Sie schnell. (There is an accident on the street; please come quickly.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Es gibt* = "There is / exists" (impersonal expression strictly requiring Accusative).
  • *einen Unfall* = "an accident" (masculine accusative indefinite article *einen* + noun *Unfall*).
  • *auf der Straße* = "on the street" (two-way preposition *auf* + feminine dative *der Straße* for static location).
  • *bitte kommen Sie schnell* = "please come quickly" (courtesy marker + formal imperative *kommen Sie* + adverb *schnell*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "It gives an accident on the street, please come you quickly."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Fixed impersonal construction "es gibt" ("there is/are") which strictly governs the Accusative case ("einen Unfall", masculine accusative indefinite article "einen").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** One of the most important grammar rules tested at A1: "es gibt" ALWAYS takes the Accusative case, never the Nominative.

**#202. Ich habe meine Tasche verloren.** — I lost my bag.
*Example sentence:* Ich habe meine Tasche im Bus verloren. (I lost my bag on the bus.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich habe ... verloren* = "I have lost" (Perfekt frame).
  • *meine Tasche* = "my bag" (feminine accusative possessive *meine* + noun *Tasche*).
  • *im Bus* = "on / in the bus" (contracted *in dem Bus*, masculine dative).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I have my bag in-the bus lost."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Perfekt tense with feminine singular noun in Accusative: possessive article ending "-e" ("meine Tasche") + Partizip II "verloren" at clause end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard lost-and-found scenario in Hören Teil 1 and Sprechen Teil 2 when inquiring at station counters or bus depots.

**#203. Können Sie die Feuerwehr rufen?** — Can you call the fire department?
*Example sentence:* Können Sie die Feuerwehr rufen? Es brennt! (Can you call the fire department? There's a fire!)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Können Sie* = "Can you" (formal polite modal request).
  • *die Feuerwehr* = "the fire brigade / fire department" (feminine accusative direct object, lit. "fire-defense").
  • *rufen?* = "call?" (infinitive at clause end completing modal bracket).
  • *Es brennt!* = "There is a fire! / It's burning!" (impersonal subject *es* + 3rd person singular of *brennen*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Can you the fire-defense call? It burns!"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Polite modal request: modal verb *können* (formal "Können Sie", position 1) + feminine accusative direct object ("die Feuerwehr") + base infinitive "rufen" at the final clause position.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Goethe A1 Sprechen Teil 3 prompt card with picture of fire/flames. The expected candidate action is to formulate a polite request starting with "Können Sie bitte...".

**#204. Mir ist schwindelig.** — I feel dizzy.
*Example sentence:* Mir ist schwindelig, ich muss mich hinsetzen. (I feel dizzy; I need to sit down.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Mir ist schwindelig* = "I feel dizzy" (dative pronoun *mir* + 3rd person singular copula *ist* + adjective *schwindelig*).
  • *ich muss* = "I must / need to" (modal verb *müssen*).
  • *mich hinsetzen* = "sit down" (reflexive pronoun in accusative *mich* + separable verb in infinitive *hinsetzen* at end).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "To-me is dizzy, I must myself down-sit."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Impersonal physical state expression with dative personal pronoun: "Mir" (1st person singular dative of *ich*) + 3rd person singular copula "ist" + descriptive adjective "schwindelig".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Idiomatic expression for health complaints in Sprechen Teil 2 (Arzt / Gesundheit). Note that German uses Dative ("Mir ist kalt / schlecht / schwindelig") rather than "Ich bin...".

---

## At the Post Office / Bank (#205–#215)

**#205. Ich möchte Geld abheben.** — I'd like to withdraw money.
*Example sentence:* Ich möchte Geld abheben, wo ist der Geldautomat? (I'd like to withdraw money; where is the ATM?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich möchte* = "I would like to" (polite subjunctive modal form).
  • *Geld* = "money" (uncountable direct object in Accusative).
  • *abheben* = "withdraw" (separable infinitive *abheben* at clause end).
  • *wo ist* = "where is" (interrogative adverb + copula).
  • *der Geldautomat?* = "the cash machine / ATM?" (masculine nominative subject, lit. "money-automaton").
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I would-like money off-lift (withdraw), where is the money-machine?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Polite subjunctive modal auxiliary form *möchte* (1st person singular, V2) + direct object in Accusative ("Geld") + separable infinitive verb ("abheben") at the end of the sentence bracket.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard banking transaction request in Sprechen Teil 2 (prompt card "Bank" or "Geld"). "Geldautomat" (ATM) is high-frequency A1 reading/listening vocabulary.

**#206. Ich möchte ein Paket schicken.** — I'd like to send a package.
*Example sentence:* Ich möchte ein Paket schicken, nach Frankreich bitte. (I'd like to send a package, to France please.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich möchte* = "I would like to".
  • *ein Paket* = "a package / parcel" (neuter accusative direct object: *das Paket* -> *ein Paket*).
  • *schicken* = "send / mail" (infinitive verb at clause end).
  • *nach Frankreich bitte* = "to France please" (directional preposition *nach* for countries without article + country name + polite marker).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I would-like a parcel send, to France please."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Polite modal *möchte* (V2) + neuter accusative direct object ("ein Paket") + transitive infinitive verb "schicken" at final position.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Fundamental postal service opening request at the counter (Postfiliale) in Sprechen Teil 2/3 and Hören Teil 1 service dialogues.

**#207. Briefmarken, bitte.** — Stamps, please.
*Example sentence:* Zehn Briefmarken, bitte, für Postkarten. (Ten stamps, please, for postcards.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Zehn* = "Ten" (cardinal number).
  • *Briefmarken* = "postage stamps" (plural of *die Briefmarke*, lit. "letter-marks").
  • *bitte* = "please" (courtesy marker).
  • *für Postkarten* = "for postcards" (accusative preposition *für* + plural noun *Postkarten*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Ten letter-marks, please, for postcards."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Elliptical counter request: plural noun "Briefmarken" (*die Briefmarke*, plural ending "-n") + polite particle "bitte".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential shorthand for quick over-the-counter purchases in everyday German and A1 listening comprehension tasks.

**#208. Ich möchte ein Konto eröffnen.** — I'd like to open an account.
*Example sentence:* Ich möchte ein Konto eröffnen, was brauche ich dafür? (I'd like to open an account; what do I need for that?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich möchte* = "I would like to".
  • *ein Konto* = "a bank account" (neuter accusative direct object: *das Konto* -> *ein Konto*).
  • *eröffnen* = "open / inaugurate" (inseparable infinitive verb with prefix *er-*).
  • *was brauche ich* = "what do I need" (W-word *was* + verb *brauche* + subject *ich*).
  • *dafür?* = "for that / for it?" (pronominal adverb *da-* + *für*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I would-like an account open, what need I therefor?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal auxiliary *möchte* (V2) + neuter accusative noun phrase ("ein Konto") + inseparable prefix verb in infinitive ("eröffnen", prefix *er-*) at the final bracket position.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common transactional phrase tested in reading comprehension (bank brochures / flyers) and speaking roleplays for new arrivals.

**#209. Wie viel kostet das Porto?** — How much is the postage?
*Example sentence:* Wie viel kostet das Porto für einen Brief nach Italien? (How much is the postage for a letter to Italy?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wie viel* = "How much" (interrogative phrase for price/quantity).
  • *kostet* = "costs" (3rd person singular verb).
  • *das Porto* = "the postage / postal fee" (neuter nominative subject).
  • *für einen Brief* = "for a letter" (preposition *für* + masculine accusative *einen Brief*).
  • *nach Italien* = "to Italy" (destination preposition *nach* + country name).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "How much costs the postage for a letter to Italy?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Interrogative price phrase "Wie viel" (position 1) + verb *kosten* ("kostet", V2) + neuter subject in Nominative ("das Porto").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Core pricing question for post office scenarios in Sprechen Teil 2 when asking about letter or parcel rates.

**#210. Ich möchte Geld überweisen.** — I'd like to transfer money.
*Example sentence:* Ich möchte Geld überweisen, hier ist die Kontonummer. (I'd like to transfer money; here is the account number.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich möchte* = "I would like to".
  • *Geld* = "money" (direct object in Accusative).
  • *überweisen* = "transfer (money)" (inseparable infinitive verb).
  • *hier ist* = "here is" (local adverb *hier* + copula *ist*).
  • *die Kontonummer* = "the account number" (compound feminine noun: *das Konto* + *die Nummer*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I would-like money over-remit (transfer), here is the account-number."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal auxiliary *möchte* (V2) + direct object "Geld" + inseparable prefix verb in infinitive "überweisen" (accent on root "-weis-") at the sentence end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Practical banking vocabulary often encountered on banking forms (Überweisungsformular) and online banking interfaces in Lesen Teil 3.

**#211. Wann kommt das Paket an?** — When will the package arrive?
*Example sentence:* Wann kommt das Paket an? Ich warte schon eine Woche. (When will the package arrive? I've been waiting a week already.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wann kommt ... an?* = "When arrives / will arrive?" (question word *wann* + separable verb stem *kommt* + prefix *an* at end).
  • *das Paket* = "the package" (neuter nominative subject).
  • *Ich warte* = "I wait / I have been waiting" (German simple present denotes ongoing action when paired with *schon*).
  • *schon eine Woche* = "already for a week" (adverb *schon* + accusative time duration *eine Woche*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "When comes the package on (arrive)? I wait already one week."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Interrogative temporal adverb "Wann" (position 1) + conjugated separable verb stem "kommt" (V2) + neuter subject "das Paket" + detached separable prefix "an" at the end of the question clause.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Demonstrates the separable verb rule: "ankommen" splits into "kommt ... an" in main clauses and W-questions.

**#212. Ich möchte einen Einschreibebrief senden.** — I'd like to send a registered letter.
*Example sentence:* Ich möchte einen Einschreibebrief senden, das ist sehr wichtig. (I'd like to send a registered letter; it's very important.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich möchte* = "I would like to".
  • *einen Einschreibebrief* = "a registered letter" (masculine accusative article *einen* + noun *Einschreibebrief*).
  • *senden* = "send" (infinitive at clause end).
  • *das ist sehr wichtig* = "that is very important" (demonstrative subject *das* + *ist* + degree adverb *sehr* + adjective *wichtig*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I would-like an enrollment-letter send, that is very important."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal auxiliary *möchte* (V2) + compound masculine noun in Accusative ("einen Einschreibebrief", *der Brief* -> *einen Brief*) + infinitive "senden" at clause end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Demonstrates masculine accusative article inflection combined with specialized postal vocabulary for important documents.

**#213. Kann ich mit Karte bezahlen?** — Can I pay by card?
*Example sentence:* Kann ich mit Karte bezahlen oder nur bar? (Can I pay by card or only cash?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Kann ich* = "Can I" (modal verb *können* + subject *ich*).
  • *mit Karte* = "with / by card" (dative preposition *mit* + noun *Karte* without article).
  • *bezahlen* = "pay" (infinitive verb at end).
  • *oder nur bar?* = "or only cash?" (conjunction *oder* + adverb *nur* + adverb *bar* meaning in cash).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Can I with card pay or only cash?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Verb-first yes/no question with modal verb *können* ("Kann", 1st person singular) + subject "ich" + prepositional phrase with dative preposition "mit" ("mit Karte") + infinitive "bezahlen" at final position.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Extremely practical everyday phrase across shops, restaurants, postal counters, and ticket desks. Very common in Sprechen Teil 3.

**#214. Ich brauche einen Kontoauszug.** — I need a bank statement.
*Example sentence:* Ich brauche einen Kontoauszug für die letzten drei Monate. (I need a bank statement for the last three months.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich brauche* = "I need".
  • *einen Kontoauszug* = "a bank statement / account statement" (masculine accusative article *einen* + compound noun *Kontoauszug*).
  • *für* = "for" (preposition governing Accusative).
  • *die letzten drei Monate* = "the last three months" (plural accusative definite article *die* + inflected adjective *letzten* + number *drei* + plural noun *Monate*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I need an account-extract for the last three months."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Transitive verb *brauchen* (V2) + masculine direct object in Accusative ("einen Kontoauszug", taking the accusative ending "-en").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common paperwork requirement in administrative, immigration, and banking contexts in German-speaking countries.

**#215. Wo kann ich Geld wechseln?** — Where can I exchange money?
*Example sentence:* Wo kann ich Geld wechseln? Ich habe nur Dollar. (Where can I exchange money? I only have dollars.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wo kann ich* = "Where can I" (W-word *wo* + modal *kann* + subject *ich*).
  • *Geld wechseln?* = "exchange money?" (direct object *Geld* + infinitive *wechseln* at clause end).
  • *Ich habe* = "I have".
  • *nur Dollar* = "only dollars" (adverb *nur* + currency noun *Dollar*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Where can I money exchange? I have only dollars."
- **Grammar & Structure:** W-question: interrogative local adverb "Wo" (position 1) + modal auxiliary "kann" (V2) + subject "ich" + direct object "Geld" + infinitive "wechseln" at the end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential travel and banking question for foreign currency exchange in Sprechen Teil 2.

---

## Daily Routine (#216–#227)

**#216. Ich stehe um 7 Uhr auf.** — I get up at 7 o'clock.
*Example sentence:* Ich stehe um 7 Uhr auf und dusche mich sofort. (I get up at 7 o'clock and shower right away.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich stehe ... auf* = "I get up / stand up" (separable verb *aufstehen*: stem *stehe* at V2 + prefix *auf* at clause end).
  • *um 7 Uhr* = "at 7 o'clock" (temporal preposition *um* for exact clock time).
  • *und dusche mich* = "and shower (myself)" (conjunction *und* + verb *dusche* + accusative reflexive pronoun *mich*).
  • *sofort* = "immediately / right away" (temporal adverb).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I stand at 7 clock up and shower myself immediately."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Separable verb *aufstehen*: conjugated stem "stehe" at V2, subject "ich" at position 1, separable prefix "auf" positioned at the very end of the main clause. Temporal preposition "um" indicates exact clock time.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Cornerstone phrase for describing your daily schedule (Tagesablauf) in Sprechen Teil 1/2 and Schreiben Teil 1.

**#217. Ich frühstücke um halb acht.** — I have breakfast at half past seven.
*Example sentence:* Ich frühstücke um halb acht, meistens Brot und Kaffee. (I have breakfast at half past seven, usually bread and coffee.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich frühstücke* = "I have breakfast / eat breakfast" (verb *frühstücken*).
  • *um halb acht* = "at 7:30" (German *halb acht* literally means "halfway to eight", which corresponds to 7:30).
  • *meistens* = "mostly / usually" (frequency adverb).
  • *Brot und Kaffee* = "bread and coffee" (food items with zero article).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I breakfast at half eight (7:30), mostly bread and coffee."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Regular verb *frühstücken* ("frühstücke", V2) + clock time phrase with "halb" ("halb acht" = 7:30, literally "halfway towards eight").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Major listening trap in Goethe A1 Hören Teil 1: in German, "halb acht" means 7:30 (NOT 8:30). Always subtract 30 minutes from the named hour.

**#218. Ich gehe ins Bett um 22 Uhr.** — I go to bed at 10 p.m.
*Example sentence:* Ich gehe ins Bett um 22 Uhr, weil ich müde bin. (I go to bed at 10 p.m. because I'm tired.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich gehe* = "I go".
  • *ins Bett* = "into bed / to bed" (contracted *in das Bett*, neuter accusative showing movement).
  • *um 22 Uhr* = "at 10:00 PM" (24-hour military/official time used in German).
  • *weil ich müde bin* = "because I am tired" (subordinating conjunction *weil* kicks conjugated verb *bin* to the end of the subordinate clause).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I go into-the bed at 22 clock, because I tired am."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Verb of movement *gehen* (V2) + directional two-way preposition contraction in Accusative ("ins Bett" = *in das Bett*) + temporal prepositional phrase ("um 22 Uhr").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Official German time uses the 24-hour clock (22 Uhr = 10:00 PM), which is tested extensively in Hören and Lesen.

**#219. Ich putze mir die Zähne.** — I brush my teeth.
*Example sentence:* Ich putze mir die Zähne nach dem Frühstück. (I brush my teeth after breakfast.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich putze mir* = "I brush for myself" (verb *putzen* + 1st person singular dative reflexive pronoun *mir*).
  • *die Zähne* = "the teeth" (plural direct object in Accusative; German uses definite article rather than English possessive "my").
  • *nach dem Frühstück* = "after breakfast" (dative preposition *nach* + neuter dative article *dem* + noun *Frühstück*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I brush to-me the teeth after the breakfast."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Reflexive construction with dative reflexive pronoun for body parts: transitive verb *putzen* (V2) + reflexive pronoun in Dative ("mir") + definite direct object in Accusative ("die Zähne").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Demonstrates German body care grammar: German uses Dative reflexive pronoun (*mir*) + definite article (*die Zähne*) rather than possessive determiners ("my teeth").

**#220. Ich fahre mit dem Bus zur Arbeit.** — I take the bus to work.
*Example sentence:* Ich fahre mit dem Bus zur Arbeit, das dauert 20 Minuten. (I take the bus to work; it takes 20 minutes.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich fahre* = "I travel / ride / go" (verb *fahren*).
  • *mit dem Bus* = "by bus" (dative preposition *mit* + masculine article *dem* + noun *Bus*).
  • *zur Arbeit* = "to work" (contracted preposition *zu der*, feminine dative + noun *Arbeit*).
  • *das dauert* = "that takes / lasts" (subject *das* + verb *dauern*).
  • *20 Minuten* = "20 minutes" (duration in Accusative).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I ride with the bus to-the work, that lasts 20 minutes."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Strong verb *fahren* (V2) + transportation phrase with dative preposition "mit" ("mit dem Bus", *mit* always demands Dative) + contracted directional prepositional phrase ("zur Arbeit" = *zu der Arbeit*, feminine dative).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard response in Sprechen Teil 1 and Teil 2 for questions regarding daily commute, work travel, and public transit.

**#221. Ich koche das Abendessen.** — I cook dinner.
*Example sentence:* Ich koche das Abendessen für meine Familie. (I cook dinner for my family.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich koche* = "I cook".
  • *das Abendessen* = "the dinner / evening meal" (neuter accusative direct object, lit. "evening-food").
  • *für meine Familie* = "for my family" (accusative preposition *für* + feminine possessive determiner *meine* + noun *Familie*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I cook the evening-meal for my family."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Regular transitive verb *kochen* (V2) + neuter accusative direct object ("das Abendessen") + prepositional phrase with accusative preposition *für* ("für meine Familie").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common daily routine and hobby phrase in Sprechen Teil 1 (interests/hobbies) and Teil 2.

**#222. Ich mache die Hausaufgaben.** — I do the homework.
*Example sentence:* Ich mache die Hausaufgaben nach der Schule. (I do the homework after school.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich mache* = "I do / make".
  • *die Hausaufgaben* = "the homework / assignments" (plural accusative direct object, lit. "house-tasks").
  • *nach der Schule* = "after school" (dative preposition *nach* + feminine dative article *der* + noun *Schule*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I make the house-tasks after the school."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Transitive verb *machen* (V2) + plural accusative direct object ("die Hausaufgaben") + temporal preposition "nach" (which always governs Dative: "nach der Schule").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Frequent student and course-participant vocabulary in A1 reading texts and daily routine descriptions.

**#223. Ich räume die Wohnung auf.** — I tidy up the apartment.
*Example sentence:* Am Samstag räume ich die Wohnung auf. (On Saturday I tidy up the apartment.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Am Samstag* = "On Saturday" (temporal phrase in position 1 causing subject-verb inversion).
  • *räume ich ... auf* = "tidy I up" (separable verb *aufräumen*: stem *räume* at V2, subject *ich*, prefix *auf* at the end).
  • *die Wohnung* = "the apartment" (feminine accusative direct object).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "On-the Saturday clean I the apartment up."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Separable verb *aufräumen*: conjugated stem "räume" at V2, direct object in Accusative ("die Wohnung"), separable prefix "auf" at the final sentence position. Note subject-verb inversion when starting with a time expression.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High-frequency separable verb in Goethe A1 exams. Remember to keep the prefix "auf" at the very end of the clause.

**#224. Ich gehe einkaufen.** — I go shopping.
*Example sentence:* Ich gehe einkaufen, wir brauchen Milch und Brot. (I go shopping; we need milk and bread.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich gehe einkaufen* = "I go shopping" (verb of motion *gehen* + bare infinitive *einkaufen*).
  • *wir brauchen* = "we need".
  • *Milch und Brot* = "milk and bread" (staple grocery items with zero article).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I go in-buy (shop), we need milk and bread."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Double-verb construction: conjugated verb of motion *gehen* (V2) + bare infinitive "einkaufen" placed at the end of the clause.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common everyday routine expression used in Sprechen Teil 3 to state errands or invite your exam partner to go shopping together.

**#225. Ich dusche mich morgens.** — I shower in the morning.
*Example sentence:* Ich dusche mich morgens, das macht mich wach. (I shower in the morning; it wakes me up.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich dusche mich* = "I shower (myself)" (reflexive verb *sich duschen* + accusative reflexive pronoun *mich*).
  • *morgens* = "in the morning / every morning" (adverb formed by adding *-s* to *Morgen*).
  • *das macht mich wach* = "that makes me alert / awake" (subject *das* + verb *macht* + direct object *mich* + predicate adjective *wach*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I shower myself mornings, that makes me awake."
- **Grammar & Structure:** True reflexive verb *sich duschen*: conjugated verb "dusche" (V2) + accusative reflexive pronoun "mich" + temporal adverb "morgens" (written in lowercase with an "-s" suffix to signify repeated/routine action).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Highlights reflexive verb usage in daily routine presentations during the A1 oral examination.

**#226. Ich wasche die Wäsche.** — I do the laundry.
*Example sentence:* Am Sonntag wasche ich die Wäsche. (On Sunday I do the laundry.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Am Sonntag* = "On Sunday" (position 1 temporal phrase causing inversion).
  • *wasche ich* = "wash I" (verb *wasche* at V2 + subject *ich* in position 3).
  • *die Wäsche* = "the laundry / clothes to be washed" (feminine accusative direct object).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "On-the Sunday wash I the wash (laundry)."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Stem-changing verb *waschen* (regular in 1st person "ich wasche"; vowel change *a* -> *ä* in 2nd/3rd person: *du wäschst, er wäscht*) + feminine accusative direct object ("die Wäsche").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Common household chore vocabulary frequently tested in listening dialogues and reading comprehension.

**#227. Ich sehe am Abend fern.** — I watch TV in the evening.
*Example sentence:* Ich sehe am Abend fern und entspanne mich. (I watch TV in the evening and relax.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich sehe ... fern* = "I watch TV" (separable verb *fernsehen*: stem *sehe* at V2, prefix *fern* at the end of the first clause).
  • *am Abend* = "in the evening" (contracted *an dem Abend*, masculine dative).
  • *und entspanne mich* = "and relax (myself)" (conjunction *und* + reflexive verb *sich entspannen* + accusative reflexive pronoun *mich*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I see in-the evening far (watch TV) and relax myself."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Separable irregular verb *fernsehen*: conjugated stem "sehe" at V2, temporal prepositional phrase "am Abend" in the middle field, detached prefix "fern" at the end bracket. Remember vowel shift in 2nd/3rd person: *du siehst fern, er sieht fern*.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High-frequency verb for leisure and daily routine. Keep the prefix "fern" at the end of the sentence.

---

## Polite Requests and Apologies (#228–#239)

**#228. Entschuldigung, wo ist die Toilette?** — Excuse me, where is the restroom?
*Example sentence:* Entschuldigung, wo ist die Toilette? Ich finde sie nicht. (Excuse me, where is the restroom? I can't find it.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Entschuldigung* = "Excuse me / Apologies" (polite attention-getting interjection).
  • *wo ist* = "where is" (local interrogative adverb + copula).
  • *die Toilette?* = "the restroom / toilet?" (feminine nominative subject).
  • *Ich finde sie nicht* = "I cannot find it / I don't find it" (subject *ich* + verb *finde* + feminine accusative pronoun *sie* referring back to *die Toilette* + negation *nicht*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Excuse, where is the toilet? I find her (it) not."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Polite introductory interjection "Entschuldigung" + interrogative local adverb "wo" (position 1) + copula "ist" (V2) + feminine nominative subject ("die Toilette").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** The single most useful practical question for travel and public survival. Universally accepted polite attention-getter in public spaces.

**#229. Könnten Sie mir helfen?** — Could you help me?
*Example sentence:* Könnten Sie mir helfen? Ich finde den Weg nicht. (Could you help me? I can't find the way.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Könnten Sie* = "Could you" (polite Konjunktiv II form of *können* + formal pronoun *Sie*).
  • *mir helfen?* = "help me?" (dative pronoun *mir* governed by infinitive *helfen*).
  • *Ich finde* = "I find".
  • *den Weg nicht* = "not the way / cannot find my way" (masculine accusative article *den* + noun *Weg* + negation *nicht*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Could you to-me help? I find the way not."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Polite Konjunktiv II question: auxiliary "Könnten" (position 1) + formal subject "Sie" + personal pronoun in Dative ("mir", governed by the verb *helfen*) + infinitive verb "helfen" at the end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** High-scoring polite request structure for Goethe A1 Sprechen Teil 3 (asking your partner for help with a task or direction).

**#230. Es tut mir leid, ich bin zu spät.** — I'm sorry, I'm late.
*Example sentence:* Es tut mir leid, ich bin zu spät, der Bus hatte Verspätung. (I'm sorry, I'm late; the bus was delayed.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Es tut mir leid* = "I am sorry" (fixed idiom: impersonal *es* + verb *tut* + dative *mir* + *leid*, lit. "it does sorrow to me").
  • *ich bin zu spät* = "I am too late" (subject *ich* + *bin* + degree adverb *zu* + adjective *spät*).
  • *der Bus hatte Verspätung* = "the bus was delayed / had a delay" (subject *der Bus* + Präteritum verb *hatte* + noun *Verspätung*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "It does to-me sorrow, I am too late, the bus had delay."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Fixed idiomatic apology formula "Es tut mir leid" (impersonal subject "Es" + verb *tun* + dative personal pronoun "mir" + predicate adjective "leid") followed by an explanatory main clause.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard apology formula required in Goethe A1 Schreiben Teil 2 when explaining a delayed arrival or missing an appointment.

**#231. Darf ich hier sitzen?** — May I sit here?
*Example sentence:* Darf ich hier sitzen? Ist der Platz frei? (May I sit here? Is this seat free?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Darf ich* = "May I / Am I permitted to" (modal verb *dürfen* expressing permission).
  • *hier sitzen?* = "sit here?" (local adverb *hier* + infinitive *sitzen* at bracket end).
  • *Ist der Platz frei?* = "Is the seat available/free?" (verb *ist* + masculine subject *der Platz* + predicate adjective *frei*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "May I here sit? Is the place free?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Modal verb of permission *dürfen* ("Darf", 1st person singular, position 1) + subject pronoun "ich" + local adverb "hier" + infinitive "sitzen" at the final bracket position.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Classic etiquette question on trains, buses, and in crowded cafés. Distinguishes *dürfen* (permission) from *können* (ability).

**#232. Könnten Sie das bitte wiederholen?** — Could you please repeat that?
*Example sentence:* Könnten Sie das bitte wiederholen? Ich habe es nicht verstanden. (Could you please repeat that? I didn't understand it.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Könnten Sie* = "Could you" (polite subjunctive request).
  • *das bitte wiederholen?* = "please repeat that?" (demonstrative *das* + polite *bitte* + infinitive *wiederholen*).
  • *Ich habe es nicht verstanden* = "I didn't understand it" (Perfekt tense: *habe* + pronoun *es* + negation *nicht* + past participle *verstanden* from *verstehen*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Could you that please repeat? I have it not understood."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Konjunktiv II polite question (*Könnten Sie*) + neuter demonstrative pronoun in Accusative ("das") + polite particle "bitte" + inseparable infinitive verb "wiederholen" at clause end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Crucial life-saving phrase during the Goethe A1 Sprechen exam if you don't hear or understand the examiner or partner. Examiners never penalize asking for repetition politely.

**#233. Verzeihen Sie die Störung.** — Forgive the disturbance.
*Example sentence:* Verzeihen Sie die Störung, ich habe eine Frage. (Forgive the disturbance; I have a question.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Verzeihen Sie* = "Forgive / Pardon" (formal imperative: verb *verzeihen* + *Sie*).
  • *die Störung* = "the disturbance / interruption" (feminine direct object in Accusative).
  • *ich habe* = "I have".
  • *eine Frage* = "a question" (feminine accusative indefinite article *eine* + noun *Frage*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Pardon you the disturbance, I have a question."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Formal imperative structure: verb *verzeihen* at position 1 + formal pronoun "Sie" + feminine direct object in Accusative ("die Störung").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Highly polite opening in professional, customer-service, or office settings before interrupting someone to ask for information.

**#234. Bitte sprechen Sie langsamer.** — Please speak more slowly.
*Example sentence:* Bitte sprechen Sie langsamer, ich lerne noch Deutsch. (Please speak more slowly; I'm still learning German.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Bitte sprechen Sie* = "Please speak" (polite marker *bitte* + formal imperative *sprechen Sie*).
  • *langsamer* = "more slowly" (comparative form of the adverb *langsam*).
  • *ich lerne* = "I am learning / study".
  • *noch Deutsch* = "still German" (adverb *noch* + language noun *Deutsch*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Please speak you slower, I learn still German."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Polite formal imperative: courtesy marker "Bitte" + imperative verb "sprechen" (position 1) + formal pronoun "Sie" + comparative adverb of manner ("langsamer").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Indispensable communication strategy phrase during the oral examination and daily conversations when native speakers talk too fast.

**#235. Das macht nichts.** — That doesn't matter. / No problem.
*Example sentence:* Das macht nichts, kein Problem! (That doesn't matter, no problem!)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Das macht nichts* = "That doesn't matter / That's alright" (subject *das* + verb *macht* + negative indefinite pronoun *nichts*, lit. "that makes nothing").
  • *kein Problem!* = "no problem!" (negative determiner *kein* + neuter noun *Problem*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "That makes nothing, no problem!"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Demonstrative subject "Das" (neuter nominative) + verb *machen* ("macht", V2) + indefinite negative pronoun "nichts".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Natural, reassuring conversational response to an apology (answering "Entschuldigung" or "Es tut mir leid").

**#236. Könnten Sie mir den Weg zeigen?** — Could you show me the way?
*Example sentence:* Könnten Sie mir den Weg zum Bahnhof zeigen? (Could you show me the way to the train station?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Könnten Sie* = "Could you" (polite Konjunktiv II modal).
  • *mir* = "to me" (dative indirect recipient pronoun).
  • *den Weg* = "the way / path" (masculine accusative direct object).
  • *zum Bahnhof* = "to the train station" (contracted *zu dem*, masculine dative).
  • *zeigen?* = "show / point out?" (infinitive at sentence end).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Could you to-me the way to-the train station show?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Konjunktiv II polite request (*Könnten Sie*) + dative recipient personal pronoun ("mir") + masculine direct object in Accusative ("den Weg") + infinitive verb "zeigen" at the end.
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Exemplifies the double-object rule in German (Dative person before Accusative object). Very useful in Sprechen Teil 2 and Teil 3 for asking directions.

**#237. Entschuldigen Sie bitte, ich habe einen Fehler gemacht.** — Please excuse me, I made a mistake.
*Example sentence:* Entschuldigen Sie bitte, ich habe einen Fehler gemacht, das war meine Schuld. (Please excuse me, I made a mistake; that was my fault.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Entschuldigen Sie bitte* = "Please excuse me" (formal imperative).
  • *ich habe ... gemacht* = "I have made / I made" (Perfekt of *machen*).
  • *einen Fehler* = "a mistake / error" (masculine accusative article *einen* + noun *Fehler*).
  • *das war* = "that was" (demonstrative subject *das* + past tense copula *war*).
  • *meine Schuld* = "my fault / my blame" (feminine possessive *meine* + noun *Schuld*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Excuse you please, I have a mistake made, that was my fault."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Formal imperative ("Entschuldigen Sie bitte") followed by a Perfekt clause: auxiliary *haben* ("habe") + masculine direct object in Accusative ("einen Fehler") + past participle "gemacht".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Exemplary formula for formal written correspondence (Schreiben Teil 2) when acknowledging an error or correcting information previously sent.

**#238. Wären Sie so nett?** — Would you be so kind?
*Example sentence:* Wären Sie so nett und geben mir das Salz? (Would you be so kind and pass me the salt?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wären Sie so nett* = "Would you be so kind" (Konjunktiv II of *sein*: *wären* + formal pronoun *Sie* + adverb *so* + adjective *nett*).
  • *und geben mir* = "and give/pass me" (conjunction *und* + verb *geben* + dative pronoun *mir*).
  • *das Salz?* = "the salt?" (neuter accusative direct object).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Were you so kind and give to-me the salt?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Konjunktiv II of copula *sein*: "Wären" (position 1) + formal subject "Sie" + degree adverb "so" + predicate adjective "nett".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Advanced polite formula that demonstrates elevated register and politeness during Sprechen Teil 3 requests.

**#239. Vielen Dank für Ihre Hilfe!** — Thank you very much for your help!
*Example sentence:* Vielen Dank für Ihre Hilfe, das war sehr nett von Ihnen! (Thank you very much for your help; that was very kind of you!)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Vielen Dank* = "Many thanks / Thank you very much" (masculine accusative greeting formula).
  • *für Ihre Hilfe* = "for your help" (preposition *für* + formal possessive in feminine Accusative *Ihre* + noun *Hilfe*).
  • *das war* = "that was".
  • *sehr nett von Ihnen!* = "very kind of you!" (adverb *sehr* + adjective *nett* + dative preposition *von* + formal dative pronoun *Ihnen*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Many thanks for your help, that was very kind of you!"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Fixed accusative expression "Vielen Dank" + preposition "für" (which strictly governs Accusative) + formal possessive determiner in feminine Accusative ("Ihre Hilfe").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Essential closing formula for formal emails in Schreiben Teil 2 and warm concluding remarks in Sprechen Teil 3 interactions.

---

## Numbers, Counting, and Basic Quantities (#240–#250)

**#240. Einmal Kaffee, bitte.** — One coffee, please.
*Example sentence:* Einmal Kaffee, bitte, mit Milch. (One coffee, please, with milk.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Einmal* = "One time / One serving of" (multiplicative counting adverb).
  • *Kaffee* = "coffee" (masculine noun, zero article).
  • *bitte* = "please" (courtesy marker).
  • *mit Milch* = "with milk" (dative preposition *mit* + uncountable noun *Milch*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "One-time coffee, please, with milk."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Multiplicative adverb "Einmal" ("once / one portion") + masculine noun "Kaffee" (uncountable / zero article) + polite particle "bitte".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard colloquial ordering formula in bakeries, cafés, and food stands throughout Germany, Austria, and Switzerland.

**#241. Zweimal Fahrkarten nach München.** — Two tickets to Munich.
*Example sentence:* Zweimal Fahrkarten nach München, hin und zurück. (Two tickets to Munich, round trip.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Zweimal* = "Two times / Two tickets" (multiplicative counting adverb).
  • *Fahrkarten* = "tickets / travel tickets" (plural of *die Fahrkarte*).
  • *nach München* = "to Munich" (directional preposition *nach* + city name).
  • *hin und zurück* = "round trip / there and back" (idiom: *hin* = there, *zurück* = back).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Two-times travel-tickets to Munich, there and back."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Multiplicative numeral "Zweimal" ("twice / two of") + plural noun in Accusative ("Fahrkarten") + destination prepositional phrase ("nach München").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Standard ticketing phrasing at train station service desks (Reisezentrum / Schalter) in Sprechen Teil 2 and Hören Teil 1 dialogues.

**#242. Ein Kilo Äpfel, bitte.** — One kilo of apples, please.
*Example sentence:* Ein Kilo Äpfel, bitte, die roten dort. (One kilo of apples, please, the red ones there.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ein Kilo* = "One kilogram" (neuter unit of mass: *das Kilo*).
  • *Äpfel* = "apples" (plural of *der Apfel* in apposition).
  • *bitte* = "please".
  • *die roten dort* = "the red ones there" (definite plural article *die* + inflected adjective *roten* + local adverb *dort*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "One kilo apples, please, the red there."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Neuter unit of measurement "Ein Kilo" (*das Kilo*) + plural substance noun in apposition ("Äpfel", zero article) + polite particle "bitte".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Classic grocery and farmers' market dialogue (auf dem Markt) in Sprechen Teil 2 (prompt card with pictures of fruit or vegetables).

**#243. Einen halben Liter Milch, bitte.** — Half a liter of milk, please.
*Example sentence:* Einen halben Liter Milch, bitte, das reicht für heute. (Half a liter of milk, please; that's enough for today.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Einen halben Liter* = "Half a liter" (masculine accusative indefinite article *einen* + mixed adjective ending *-en* on *halben* + unit noun *Liter*).
  • *Milch* = "milk" (uncountable substance noun).
  • *bitte* = "please".
  • *das reicht* = "that is enough / suffices" (subject *das* + verb *reichen*).
  • *für heute* = "for today" (preposition *für* + adverb *heute*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "A half liter milk, please, that suffices for today."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Masculine quantity in Accusative: indefinite article with accusative ending "-en" ("Einen"), mixed adjective inflection ending in "-en" ("halben"), masculine unit noun ("Liter") + uncountable substance noun ("Milch").
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Tests mastery of masculine accusative adjective endings (*einen halben Liter*) when specifying liquid volumes in food shops.

**#244. Ich hätte gern drei Brötchen.** — I'd like three bread rolls.
*Example sentence:* Ich hätte gern drei Brötchen, bitte. (I'd like three bread rolls, please.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich hätte gern* = "I would gladly have / I'd like" (Konjunktiv II *hätte* + modal adverb *gern*).
  • *drei Brötchen* = "three bread rolls" (cardinal number *drei* + plural neuter noun *Brötchen*).
  • *bitte* = "please".
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I would-have gladly three little-breads, please."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Polite Konjunktiv II construction "hätte gern" (1st person singular, V2) + cardinal number "drei" + neuter diminutive plural noun ("Brötchen", diminutive suffix "-chen" always makes nouns neuter with zero change in plural).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** The most common polite ordering formula at the bakery (Bäckerei) in Goethe A1 Sprechen Teil 2.

**#245. Das kostet fünf Euro fünfzig.** — That costs five euros fifty.
*Example sentence:* Das kostet fünf Euro fünfzig, bezahlen Sie bar? (That costs five euros fifty; are you paying cash?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Das kostet* = "That costs" (subject demonstrative pronoun *das* + verb *kostet*).
  • *fünf Euro fünfzig* = "five euros fifty (cents)" (€5.50; note *Cent* is omitted in everyday speech).
  • *bezahlen Sie bar?* = "are you paying cash?" (verb *bezahlen* + formal subject *Sie* + adverb *bar* meaning in cash).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "That costs five Euro fifty, pay you cash?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject demonstrative pronoun "Das" + 3rd person singular verb *kosten* ("kostet", V2) + compound currency amount ("fünf Euro fünfzig" = €5.50).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Price comprehension is tested in nearly every Goethe A1 Hören Teil 1 & 2 question. In spoken German, the word "Cent" is typically omitted after the Euro amount.

**#246. Ich bin dreißig Jahre alt.** — I am thirty years old.
*Example sentence:* Ich bin dreißig Jahre alt und komme aus Spanien. (I am thirty years old and come from Spain.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Ich bin* = "I am" (copula verb *sein*).
  • *dreißig Jahre alt* = "thirty years old" (number + plural noun *Jahre* + predicate adjective *alt*).
  • *und komme* = "and come" (conjunction *und* + verb *kommen*).
  • *aus Spanien* = "from Spain" (origin preposition *aus* + country name without article).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "I am thirty years old and come from Spain."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject "Ich" + copula *sein* ("bin", V2) + cardinal number ("dreißig") + plural noun "Jahre" + predicate adjective "alt".
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Mandatory personal introduction item in Goethe A1 Sprechen Teil 1 (Alter / age). Remember: German uses *sein* ("Ich bin ... Jahre alt"), never *haben*.

**#247. Wir sind zu viert.** — There are four of us.
*Example sentence:* Wir sind zu viert, haben Sie einen Tisch? (There are four of us; do you have a table?)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Wir sind zu viert* = "There are four of us / We are a party of four" (idiomatic German phrasing for group size: *zu* + inflected numeral *viert*).
  • *haben Sie* = "do you have" (formal question).
  • *einen Tisch?* = "a table?" (masculine accusative direct object: indefinite article *einen* + noun *Tisch*).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "We are to four, have you a table?"
- **Grammar & Structure:** Subject pronoun "Wir" + copula *sein* ("sind", V2) + fixed group idiom "zu" + inflected numeral in dative ("zu viert", analogous to *zu zweit, zu dritt*).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Crucial idiomatic phrase when entering a restaurant to state party size. Do not translate literally as "we are four"; German idiomatically requires "Wir sind zu viert".

**#248. Hundert Gramm Käse, bitte.** — A hundred grams of cheese, please.
*Example sentence:* Hundert Gramm Käse, bitte, von dem Gouda. (A hundred grams of cheese, please, of the Gouda.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Hundert Gramm* = "A hundred grams" (cardinal number + neuter metric unit *Gramm*).
  • *Käse* = "cheese" (masculine noun, zero article).
  • *bitte* = "please".
  • *von dem Gouda* = "of the Gouda (cheese)" (dative preposition *von* + masculine dative article *dem* + specific cheese name).
  • *Literal & Natural Meaning:* Lit. "Hundred gram cheese, please, from the Gouda."
- **Grammar & Structure:** Cardinal number "Hundert" + neuter unit noun "Gramm" (*das Gramm*) + partitive substance noun with zero article ("Käse", masculine).
- **Exam & Usage Tip:** Key quantity unit for deli and cheese counters (Käsetheke / Fleischtheke) in grocery store listening and speaking tasks.

**#249. Meine Telefonnummer ist 0170-1234567.** — My phone number is 0170-1234567.
*Example sentence:* Meine Telefonnummer ist 0170-1234567, rufen Sie mich an. (My phone number is 0170-1234567; call me.)
- **Sentence Breakdown & Meaning:**
  • *Meine Telefonnummer* = "My phone number" (feminine possessive determiner *meine* + compound noun *Telefonnummer*).
  • *ist* = "is" (copula).
  • *0170-1234567* = pronounced digit by digit: *null-eins-sieben-null-eins-zwei-drei-vier-fünf-sechs-sieben*.
